title:	Sparse checkout fails with glob patterns from brief include_patterns
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	30
--
## Step
`step-03-extract.md` — Section 4, Remote Source Resolution (Ephemeral shallow clone)

## Problem
The workflow instructs using `git sparse-checkout set {include_patterns}` with patterns from the brief like `cognee/**/*.py`, but `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns. It fails with:

```
fatal: specify directories rather than patterns. If your directory really has any of '*?[]\' in it, pass --skip-checks
```

Additionally, individual files like `pyproject.toml` also fail because sparse-checkout expects directories:
```
fatal: 'pyproject.toml' is not a directory; to treat it as a directory anyway, rerun with --skip-checks
```

## Workaround Used
Stripped globs to directory roots (`cognee/**/*.py` → `cognee`), then used `--skip-checks` to include individual files like `pyproject.toml`.

## Suggested Fix
The workflow should instruct converting `include_patterns` to unique directory roots before passing to `sparse-checkout set`. For individual files, use `--skip-checks`. Example transformation:

```
Brief include_patterns:        Sparse checkout args:
cognee/**/*.py            →    cognee
cognee-mcp/**/*.py        →    cognee-mcp
examples/**/*.py          →    examples
pyproject.toml            →    (use --skip-checks)
```

## Impact
**Execution failure** — clone step fails without workaround, blocking AST extraction.
```

> AGENT

Got it. This is about the extraction workflow step that uses `git sparse-checkout` with glob patterns from the brief. Let me load the target file.

**Here's the current content of `step-03-extract.md`:**

The issue is in **Section 4 — Remote Source Resolution**, specifically lines 96-101. The workflow instructs:

```
git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {include_patterns}
```

But `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns like `cognee/**/*.py`. Individual files like `pyproject.toml` also fail because sparse-checkout expects directories.

**What needs to change:**

The sparse-checkout instruction (lines 96-101) should be updated to:
1. Convert glob `include_patterns` to unique directory roots before passing to `sparse-checkout set`
2. Use `--skip-checks` to support individual file entries

Here's the fix:

> AGENT

**Here's what changed:**

**Before:**
```
If `include_patterns` are specified in the brief, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:

   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}
   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {include_patterns}
```

**After:**
```
If `include_patterns` are specified in the brief, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:

   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}

   **IMPORTANT:** `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns.
   Convert `include_patterns` before passing them:

   - Strip glob suffixes to directory roots (e.g., `src/core/**/*.py` → `src/core`, `src/api/*.ts` → `src/api`)
   - Deduplicate the resulting directory list
   - For individual files (e.g., `pyproject.toml`), pass `--skip-checks` to allow non-directory entries

   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}

   Example transformation:
   Brief include_patterns:        sparse-checkout args:
   src/core/**/*.py          →    src/core
   src/api/**/*.ts           →    src/api
   examples/**/*.py          →    examples
   pyproject.toml            →    pyproject.toml (with --skip-checks)

   After checkout, apply the original glob `include_patterns` as file-level filters when building
   the extraction file list — sparse-checkout gets the right directories, glob filtering narrows
   to the exact files.
```

**Are you happy with this change?**

- **[Y]es** — Keep the change
- **[N]o** — Revert and redo
- **[M]odify** — Make further adjustments

     8→# Step 3: Extract
     9→
    10→## STEP GOAL:
    11→
    12→To extract all public exports, function signatures, type definitions, and co-import patterns from the source code using tier-appropriate tools, building a complete extraction inventory with confidence-tiered provenance citations.
    13→
    14→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    15→
    16→### Universal Rules:
    17→
    18→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    19→- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file

    24→
    25→- ✅ You are a skill compilation engine performing structural extraction
    26→- ✅ Zero hallucination tolerance — every extracted item must trace to source code
    27→- ✅ Confidence tier labeling is mandatory for every extraction result
    28→
    29→### Step-Specific Rules:
    30→
    31→- 🎯 Focus ONLY on extracting exports, signatures, types from source code
    32→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to compile SKILL.md content — that's step-05
    33→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to write any output files — extraction stays in context
    34→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to include content that cannot be cited to a source location
    35→- ⚒️ Every extracted item MUST have a provenance citation: `[AST:{file}:L{line}]` or `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]`
    36→
    37→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    38→
    39→- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    40→- 💾 Build extraction inventory in context — do not write files
    41→- 📖 Load extraction patterns data file for tier-specific strategy
    42→- 🚫 If an export cannot be verified, exclude it — do not guess
    43→
    44→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    45→
    46→- Available: brief_data, tier, source_location, file_tree from step-01; ecosystem_status from step-02
    47→- Focus: Source code extraction and inventory building
    48→- Limits: Do NOT compile, assemble, or write any output
    49→- Dependencies: Source code must be accessible (resolved in step-01)
    50→
    51→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    52→
    53→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    54→
    55→### 1. Load Extraction Patterns
    56→
    57→Load `{extractionPatternsData}` completely. Identify the strategy for the current forge tier.
    58→
    59→### 2. Apply Scope Filters
    60→
    61→From the brief, apply scope and pattern filters:
    62→
    63→- `scope` — determines what to extract (e.g., "all public exports", specific modules)
    64→- `include_patterns` — file globs to include (if specified)
    65→- `exclude_patterns` — file globs to exclude (if specified)
    66→

    70→

    76→
    77→**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue with extraction below.
    78→
    79→### 4. Execute Tier-Dependent Extraction
    80→
    81→**Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep only):**
    82→
    83→If `source_repo` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
    84→
    85→If `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:

## Review: `step-03-extract.md` — sparse-checkout glob fix

Reviewing `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` (lines 96–121) and all related files in `src/`.

---

## Critical Issues

### 1. `--skip-checks` is applied globally but the instruction implies it is conditional — an agent will misread this

**Confidence: 90**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`, lines 106 and 109

The bullet at line 106 says:

> "For individual files (e.g., `pyproject.toml`), pass `--skip-checks` to allow non-directory entries"

This implies `--skip-checks` is conditional — only used when individual files are present. But the single command template on line 109 always includes it:

```
git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}
```

An AI agent following the bullet literally will produce two different commands: one without `--skip-checks` (directories only case) and one with it (mixed case). But the template shows only one form with the flag always present. An agent that trusts the prose over the code block will omit `--skip-checks` in the directories-only case and may get a git error on older git versions where the flag is unnecessary noise. An agent that trusts the code block over the prose will always use `--skip-checks`, which is actually safe but contradicts the explanation.

More critically: `{individual_files}` is a template placeholder that will be empty in the common case where `include_patterns` are all globs — the agent has no rule for what to emit when there are no individual files. It could emit a trailing empty argument, which may cause the command to fail with "invalid path ''" on some git versions.

**Fix:** Either make `--skip-checks` unconditional with a note that it is a safe no-op when no bare files are present, or provide two explicit command forms:

```
# If include_patterns contains only glob patterns (no bare files):
git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {converted_directory_roots}

# If include_patterns contains any bare file entries:
git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}
```

This eliminates the ambiguity for the agent and the empty-placeholder risk.

---

### 2. The update-skill re-extract step has the same `sparse-checkout set` bug the fix was meant to address, and was not updated

**Confidence: 95**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`, line 87

```
git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {changed_files_from_manifest}
```

The change manifest entries are individual file paths (e.g., `src/core/parser.py`, `src/api/routes.ts`). These are full file paths, not directory roots. On any git version that validates sparse-checkout entries strictly, passing non-directory paths without `--skip-checks` will fail with the identical error the create-skill fix was designed to prevent.

The `extraction-patterns.md` data file at line 237 explicitly calls out that update-skill uses `sparse-checkout scoped to the changed files from the change manifest only`, but no conversion logic or `--skip-checks` flag is specified there or in the step file. This path is broken in exactly the same way the original create-skill bug was.

**Fix:** Apply the same conversion logic to `step-03-re-extract.md`. For update-skill the situation is actually simpler — the changed files are known concrete paths, not globs — so the fix is straightforward:

```
git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {changed_files_from_manifest}
```

And update `extraction-patterns.md` line 237 to reflect that `--skip-checks` is required for file-path entries.

---

## Important Issues

### 3. Bare directory patterns (no glob) in `include_patterns` are misclassified as "individual files"

**Confidence: 85**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`, lines 104–106

The conversion rule says:
- Strip glob suffixes to directory roots — e.g., `src/core/**/*.py` becomes `src/core`
- For individual files, pass `--skip-checks`

The rule for identifying "individual files" is defined only by example: `pyproject.toml`. A pattern like `src/utils/helpers.py` (a bare file path with no glob) contains a `/` — it superficially looks like a path, not a top-level file. An agent could classify it as a directory root (wrong — it is a file), or strip the filename and pass only `src/utils` (over-broad — it includes the entire directory). Neither is correct.

Likewise, a pattern like `README.md` has no glob characters and no path separator. The example only shows `pyproject.toml` as the individual file case. An agent might not generalize that any pattern with no `*` or `?` and no trailing `/` is a candidate for the individual-file path.

**Fix:** Make the classification rule explicit:

> A pattern is an individual file if it contains no glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) AND does not end with `/`. A pattern is a directory root after stripping the glob suffix if the result contains no extension (no `.` in the last path component). When in doubt, use `--skip-checks` and pass the path as-is.

---

### 4. `extraction-patterns.md` data file — the sparse-checkout conversion rule is not mentioned, creating a divergence between the authoritative data file and the step instructions

**Confidence: 85**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`, lines 232–239

The data file is the canonical reference for the ephemeral clone strategy. Line 236 says:

> "apply sparse-checkout if `include_patterns` are specified"

It does not say anything about glob-to-directory conversion, `--skip-checks`, or the two-phase approach (sparse-checkout for directories + post-checkout glob filtering). An agent loading this data file at the start of the Forge/Deep extraction step (as instructed by the step file's "Load Extraction Patterns" in section 1) will read a summary that contradicts the detailed fix in the step itself — the data file still implies you can pass `include_patterns` directly to `sparse-checkout set`.

This creates a real ambiguity: the data file is loaded first and sets the mental model; the detailed instructions come later. An agent that summarizes the data file into working memory before reading the step's IMPORTANT block may carry the wrong assumption through.

**Fix:** Update `extraction-patterns.md` point 3 to mirror the conversion rule:

> "For create-skill: use `--depth 1 --single-branch --filter=blob:none`; if `include_patterns` are specified, convert glob patterns to directory roots before passing to `sparse-checkout set` (see step-03-extract.md for the full conversion rules). Use `--skip-checks` when passing individual file paths."

---

### 5. Breaking change: the no-`include_patterns` case now uses `--sparse` at clone time with no subsequent `sparse-checkout set`, potentially checking out nothing

**Confidence: 80**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`, lines 88–100

The flow now presents two clone command variants. The first (line 93) is for the no-`include_patterns` case. The second (line 99) adds `--sparse`. The text "if `include_patterns` are specified in the brief, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope" (line 96) is the branch condition.

When `include_patterns` is absent, the agent correctly uses the first non-sparse clone — no change there. But the two commands are presented in a sequential code-block layout without a conditional keyword (`if`/`else`). The prose says "if ... use sparse-checkout" but both commands are in separate fenced blocks that look like sequential steps to some LLMs. An agent could run both commands — the sparse clone followed immediately by the non-sparse parameters block — or skip the `sparse-checkout set` entirely after a `--sparse` clone, which leaves only the repo root checked out (git sparse default with no cone set checks out nothing beyond the top level).

This is not a regression from before the fix (the previous naive form had the same structural ambiguity), but the new conversion instructions make the two-command split more pronounced and worth making unambiguous.

**Fix:** Use an explicit conditional marker in the prose structure:

```
**If `include_patterns` are NOT specified:**
git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}

**If `include_patterns` ARE specified:**
git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}
[then apply conversion + sparse-checkout set]
```

---

    43→- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    44→- 💾 Produce structured extraction results per changed file
    45→- 📖 Label every extraction with confidence tier
    46→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to hallucinate exports — only extract what exists in source

    48→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    49→
    50→- Available: change manifest from step 02, forge tier from step 01, source files
    51→- Focus: extraction only — no merge, no write

    54→
    55→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    56→
    57→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
    58→
    59→### 1. Check for Docs-Only Mode
    60→
    61→**If `source_type: "docs-only"` in the original brief or metadata:**
    62→
    63→"**Docs-only skill detected.** This skill was generated from external documentation, not source code. Re-extraction will re-fetch the original `doc_urls` to check for updated content."
    64→
    65→- Re-fetch each URL from `doc_urls` (from the brief or metadata) using whatever web fetching capability is available
    66→- Extract updated API information with T3 `[EXT:{url}]` citations
    67→- Build the updated extraction inventory from fetched content
    68→- Skip all source code extraction below — proceed directly to the merge step (section 5 or equivalent)
    69→
    70→**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue with source extraction below.
    71→
    72→### 1b. Determine Extraction Strategy by Tier
    73→
    74→**Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep only):**
    75→
    76→If `source_root` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
    77→
    78→If `source_root` (from metadata.json) is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:
    79→
    80→1. **Check `git` availability:** Verify `git` is functional (`git --version`). If `git` is not available, skip to the fallback warning below.
    81→
    82→2. **Ephemeral sparse clone:** Clone only the changed files from the change manifest to a system temp path. Note: at this point in the flow, `{source_root}` is known to be a remote URL (the local-path case was already handled above).
    83→
    84→   ```
    85→   temp_path = {system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/
    86→   git clone --depth 1 --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_root} {temp_path}
    87→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {changed_files_from_manifest}
    88→   ```
    89→
    90→   No `--branch` flag is used — the clone targets the remote's default branch, which must match the branch used during the original [CS] Create Skill run. This scopes the clone to only the files identified in step-02's change manifest, avoiding a full repository download.
    91→
    92→3. **If clone succeeds:** Update the working source path to `{temp_path}` for all subsequent AST operations in this step. Proceed with the **Forge tier** extraction strategy below. Mark `ephemeral_clone_active = true` for cleanup.
    93→
    94→4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**
    95→
    96→   ⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:**
    97→
    98→   "Ephemeral clone of `{source_root}` failed: {error}. Degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and re-run [CS] Create Skill with the local path, then re-run this update."
    99→
   100→   Override the extraction strategy to Quick tier for this run. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
   101→
   102→**Ephemeral clone cleanup:** After extraction is complete for all files in scope (whether successful or partially failed), before presenting the extraction summary (Section 5), if `ephemeral_clone_active`, delete the `{temp_path}` directory. Log: "Ephemeral source clone cleaned up." This ensures cleanup runs even if some extractions failed, as long as the step itself is still executing.
   103→
   104→**If AST tool is unavailable at Forge/Deep tier (local source):**
   105→
   106→⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:** "AST tools are unavailable — extraction will use source reading (T1-low). Run [SF] Setup Forge to detect and configure AST tools for T1 confidence."
   107→
   108→Degrade to Quick tier extraction. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
   109→
   110→**Quick tier (text pattern matching):**
   111→- Extract function/class/type names via regex patterns
   112→- Extract export statements via text matching
   113→- Confidence: T1-low (pattern-matched, not AST-verified)
   114→
   115→**Forge tier (AST structural extraction):**
   116→
   117→⚠️ **CRITICAL:** Load and follow the **AST Extraction Protocol** from `{extractionPatternsData}`. Use the decision tree based on the number of changed files: prefer MCP `find_code()` for small sets, `find_code_by_rule()` with scoped YAML rules for medium sets, and CLI `--json=stream` with line-by-line streaming for large sets. Never use `ast-grep --json` (without `=stream`) — it loads the entire result set into memory and will fail on large codebases.
   118→
   119→- Extract: function signatures, type definitions, class members, exported constants
   120→- Extract: parameter types, return types, JSDoc/docstring comments
   121→- Confidence: T1 (AST-verified structural truth)
   122→
   123→**Deep tier (AST + QMD semantic enrichment):**
   124→- Perform all Forge tier extractions (T1)
   125→- Additionally: launch a subprocess that queries qmd_bridge for temporal context on changed exports, returning T2 evidence per export
   126→- QMD provides: usage patterns, historical context, related documentation
   127→- Confidence: T1 for structural, T2 for semantic enrichment
   128→
   129→### 2. Extract Changed Files
   130→
   131→DO NOT BE LAZY — For EACH file in the change manifest with status MODIFIED, ADDED, or RENAMED, launch a subprocess that:
   132→
   133→1. Loads the source file
   134→2. Performs tier-appropriate extraction (Quick/Forge/Deep)

   139→   - Record: return type (if function/method)

   142→4. Returns structured extraction findings to parent

   144→**For DELETED files:** No extraction needed — deletions handled in merge step.
   145→
   146→**For MOVED files:** Re-extract at new location to update file:line references.
   147→
   148→### 3. Deep Tier QMD Enrichment (Conditional)
   149→
   150→**ONLY if forge_tier == Deep:**
   151→
   152→Read the `qmd_collections` registry from `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml`.
   153→
   154→Find the collection entry matching the current skill: look for an entry where `skill_name` matches the skill being updated AND `type` is `"extraction"`.
   155→

   158→1. Queries the `{skill_name}-extraction` collection for semantic context related to the export
   159→2. Searches for usage patterns, documentation references, temporal history
   160→3. Returns T2 evidence per export (usage frequency, context snippets, related concepts)

   162→**If no matching collection found in registry:**

   164→Continue without T2 enrichment — extraction still produces T1 results.
   165→
   166→**If forge_tier != Deep:** Skip this section with notice: "QMD enrichment skipped (tier: {forge_tier})"
   167→

   169→

   194→
   195→### 5. Display Extraction Summary and Auto-Proceed

     1→# Extraction Patterns by Tier
     2→
     3→## Quick Tier (No AST)
     4→
     5→Source reading via gh_bridge — infer exports from file structure and content.
     6→
     7→### Strategy
     8→1. `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` — map source structure
     9→2. Identify entry points: index files, main exports, public modules
    10→3. `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, path)` — read each entry point
    11→4. Extract: exported function names, parameter lists, return types (from signatures)
    12→5. Infer types from JSDoc, docstrings, type annotations in source
    13→
    14→### Confidence
    15→- All results: T1-low (source reading without structural verification)
    16→- No co-import detection available
    17→- No AST-backed line numbers
    18→
    19→### Supported Patterns
    20→- `export function name(...)` / `export const name = ...` (JS/TS)
    21→- `pub fn name(...)` (Rust)
    22→- `def name(...)` with `__all__` (Python)
    23→- `func Name(...)` (Go, capitalized = exported)
    24→
    25→---
    26→
    27→## Forge Tier (AST Available)
    28→
    29→Structural extraction via ast_bridge — verified exports with line-level citations.
    30→
    31→### Strategy
    32→1. Detect language from brief or file extensions
    33→2. `ast_bridge.scan_definitions(path, language)` — extract all exports
    34→3. For each export: function name, full signature, parameter types, return type, line number
    35→4. `ast_bridge.detect_co_imports(path, libraries[])` — find integration points
    36→5. Build extraction rules YAML for reproducibility
    37→
    38→### Confidence
    39→- Exported functions with full signatures: T1 (AST-verified)
    40→- Type definitions and interfaces: T1
    41→- Co-import patterns: T1
    42→- Internal/private functions: excluded (not part of public API)
    43→
    44→### ast-grep Patterns
    45→- JS/TS: `export function $NAME($$$PARAMS): $RET` / `export const $NAME`
    46→- Rust: `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET`
    47→- Python: function definitions within `__all__` list
    48→- Go: capitalized function definitions
    49→
    50→---
    51→
    52→## Deep Tier (AST + QMD)
    53→
    54→Same extraction as Forge tier. Deep tier adds enrichment in step-04, not extraction.
    55→
    56→### Strategy
    57→- Identical to Forge tier extraction
    58→- QMD enrichment happens in the next step (step-04-enrich)
    59→- Extraction results carry forward unchanged
    60→
    61→### Confidence
    62→- Extraction: same as Forge (T1)
    63→- Enrichment annotations added in step-04: T2
    64→
    65→---
    66→
    67→## AST Extraction Protocol
    68→
    69→When AST tools are available (Forge/Deep tier), follow this deterministic protocol to prevent output overflow on large codebases. The file count from step-01's file tree determines the extraction strategy.
    70→
    71→### Decision Tree
    72→
    73→Apply the first matching condition:
    74→
    75→```
    76→Files in scope ≤ 100
    77→  → Use ast-grep MCP tool: find_code(pattern, max_results=100, output_format="text")
    78→  → Parse compact text output directly into extraction inventory
    79→
    80→Files in scope 101–500
    81→  → Use ast-grep MCP tool: find_code_by_rule(yaml, max_results=150, output_format="text")
    82→  → Use scoped YAML rules (see recipes below) to filter at the AST level
    83→  → Parse compact text output into extraction inventory
    84→
    85→Files in scope > 500
    86→  → CLI streaming fallback: ast-grep --json=stream + line-by-line Python processing
    87→  → Process in directory batches, cap per-batch output
    88→  → Merge batch results into extraction inventory
    89→```
    90→
    91→### Safety Valve
    92→
    93→If any ast-grep operation (MCP or CLI) visibly causes a timeout, returns an error related to output size, or produces unexpectedly large output: immediately switch to the CLI streaming fallback with `--json=stream`. Do not retry the same approach. Note: `max_results` in the MCP tool and `| head -N` in the CLI path provide hard caps, but this safety valve covers cases where the upstream tool itself fails before returning results (e.g., OOM during JSON serialization).
    94→
    95→### MCP Tool Usage (Preferred)
    96→
    97→**Simple pattern search:**
    98→
    99→```
   100→find_code(
   101→  project_folder="{source_path}",
   102→  pattern="async def $NAME($$$PARAMS)",
   103→  language="python",
   104→  max_results=100,
   105→  output_format="text"
   106→)
   107→```
   108→
   109→**Scoped YAML rule search (for larger repos):**
   110→
   111→```
   112→find_code_by_rule(
   113→  project_folder="{source_path}",
   114→  yaml="id: public-api\nlanguage: python\nrule:\n  pattern: 'def $NAME($$$PARAMS)'\n  inside:\n    kind: module\n    stopBy: end\nconstraints:\n  NAME:\n    regex: '^[^_]'",
   115→  max_results=150,
   116→  output_format="text"
   117→)
   118→```
   119→
   120→### CLI Streaming Fallback
   121→
   122→When MCP tools are unavailable or the repo exceeds 500 files in scope, use `--json=stream` (NEVER `--json` or `--json=pretty`) with line-by-line Python processing:
   123→
   124→```bash
   125→# Note: use $$$ for variadic params in ast-grep patterns (e.g., 'def $NAME($$$PARAMS)')
   126→ast-grep -p '{pattern}' -l {language} --json=stream {path} | python3 -c "
   127→import sys, json
   128→for line in sys.stdin:
   129→    try:
   130→        m = json.loads(line)
   131→        v = m.get('metaVariables',{})
   132→        name = v.get('single',{}).get('NAME',{}).get('text','')
   133→        if name and not name.startswith('_'):
   134→            f = m.get('file','')
   135→            ln = m.get('range',{}).get('start',{}).get('line',0)+1
   136→            sig = m.get('text','').split(chr(10))[0].strip()
   137→            print(f'[AST:{f}:L{ln}] {sig}')
   138→    except: pass
   139→" | head -200
   140→```
   141→
   142→**Critical constraints:**
   143→
   144→- ALWAYS use `--json=stream` — never `--json` (loads entire array into memory)
   145→- ALWAYS process line-by-line (`for line in sys.stdin`) — never `json.load(sys.stdin)`
   146→- ALWAYS cap output with `| head -N` as a safety valve
   147→- For repos > 500 files, process in directory batches of 20-50 files each
   148→
   149→### YAML Rule Recipes by Language
   150→
   151→**Python — public functions:**
   152→
   153→```yaml
   154→id: python-public-functions
   155→language: python
   156→rule:
   157→  pattern: 'def $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
   158→  inside:
   159→    kind: module
   160→    stopBy: end
   161→constraints:
   162→  NAME:
   163→    regex: '^[^_]'
   164→```
   165→
   166→**Python — public classes:**
   167→
   168→```yaml
   169→id: python-public-classes
   170→language: python
   171→rule:
   172→  pattern: 'class $NAME($$$BASES)'
   173→  inside:
   174→    kind: module
   175→    stopBy: end
   176→constraints:
   177→  NAME:
   178→    regex: '^[^_]'
   179→```
   180→
   181→**JavaScript/TypeScript — exported functions:**
   182→
   183→```yaml
   184→id: js-exported-functions
   185→language: typescript
   186→rule:
   187→  pattern: 'export function $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
   188→```
   189→
   190→**JavaScript/TypeScript — exported constants:**
   191→
   192→```yaml
   193→id: js-exported-constants
   194→language: typescript
   195→rule:
   196→  pattern: 'export const $NAME = $VALUE'
   197→```
   198→
   199→**Rust — public functions:**
   200→
   201→```yaml
   202→id: rust-public-functions
   203→language: rust
   204→rule:
   205→  any:
   206→    - pattern: 'pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET'
   207→    - pattern: 'pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
   208→```
   209→
   210→**Go — exported functions (capitalized):**
   211→
   212→```yaml
   213→id: go-exported-functions
   214→language: go
   215→rule:
   216→  pattern: 'func $NAME($$$PARAMS) $RET'
   217→constraints:
   218→  NAME:
   219→    regex: '^[A-Z]'
   220→```
   221→
   222→---
   223→
   224→## Tier Degradation Rules
   225→
   226→### Remote Source at Forge/Deep Tier
   227→
   228→When `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) and the tier is Forge or Deep:
   229→
   230→- **ast-grep requires local files** — it cannot operate on remote URLs
   231→
   232→**Ephemeral clone strategy (preferred):**
   233→
   234→1. Check `git` availability (`git --version`). `git` is effectively guaranteed at Deep tier (via `gh` dependency) but NOT guaranteed at Forge tier.
   235→2. If `git` is available: perform an ephemeral shallow clone to a system temp path (`{system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/`).
   236→3. For create-skill: use `--depth 1 --single-branch --filter=blob:none`; apply sparse-checkout if `include_patterns` are specified.
   237→4. For update-skill: use sparse-checkout scoped to the changed files from the change manifest only. No `--branch` flag — uses the remote default branch (must match the branch used during original create-skill run).
   238→5. If clone succeeds: use the local clone path for AST extraction. All results are T1 with `[AST:...]` citations.
   239→6. Cleanup: delete the temp directory after extraction inventory is built and all data is in context. The clone never persists beyond the extraction step.
   240→
   241→**Fallback (clone fails or `git` unavailable):**
   242→
   243→- The extraction step MUST warn the user explicitly before degrading
   244→- **create-skill:** Warning must include actionable guidance — clone locally and update `source_repo` in the brief to the local path
   245→- **update-skill:** Warning must include actionable guidance — clone locally, re-run [CS] Create Skill with the local path to regenerate provenance data, then re-run the update
   246→- Extraction proceeds using Quick tier strategy (source reading via gh_bridge)
   247→- All results labeled T1-low with `[SRC:...]` citations
   248→- The degradation reason is recorded in the evidence report
   249→
   250→Silent degradation is **forbidden**. The user must always know when AST extraction was skipped and why.
   251→
   252→### AST Tool Unavailable at Forge/Deep Tier
   253→
   254→When the tier is Forge or Deep but ast-grep is not functional:
   255→
   256→- The extraction step MUST warn the user explicitly before degrading
   257→- Warning must include actionable guidance: run [SF] Setup Forge to detect tools
   258→- Extraction proceeds using Quick tier strategy
   259→- All results labeled T1-low
   260→- The degradation reason is recorded in the evidence report
   261→
   262→### Per-File AST Failure
   263→
   264→When ast-grep fails on an individual file (parse error, unsupported syntax):
   265→
   266→- Fall back to source reading for **that file only**
   267→- Other files continue with AST extraction
   268→- The affected file's results are labeled T1-low; unaffected files retain T1
   269→- Log a warning noting which file degraded and why
   270→

  "new_string": "   **If `include_patterns` are NOT specified:**\n\n   ```\n   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}\n   ```\n\n   **If `include_patterns` ARE specified**, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:\n\n   ```\n   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}\n   ```\n\n   **IMPORTANT:** `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns. Convert `include_patterns` before passing them:\n\n   **Classification rule:** A pattern is an **individual file** if it contains no glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) AND does not end with `/`. Everything else is a glob — strip it to its directory root (the path prefix before the first glob character or wildcard segment).\n\n   - Strip glob suffixes to directory roots (e.g., `src/core/**/*.py` → `src/core`, `src/api/*.ts` → `src/api`)\n   - Deduplicate the resulting directory list\n   - Individual files (e.g., `pyproject.toml`, `src/utils/helpers.py`) are kept as-is\n\n   **If only directory roots (no individual files):**\n\n   ```\n   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {converted_directory_roots}\n   ```\n\n   **If any individual files are present (or mixed):**\n\n   ```\n   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}\n   ```\n\n   Example transformation:\n   ```\n   Brief include_patterns:        sparse-checkout args:\n   src/core/**/*.py          →    src/core          (directory root)\n   src/api/*.ts              →    src/api           (directory root)\n   examples/**/*.py          →    examples          (directory root)\n   pyproject.toml            →    pyproject.toml    (individual file, needs --skip-checks)\n   src/utils/helpers.py      →    src/utils/helpers.py (individual file, needs --skip-checks)\n   ```\n\n   After checkout, apply the original glob `include_patterns` as file-level filters when building the extraction file list — sparse-checkout gets the right directories, glob filtering narrows to the exact files."
}
```

@@ -233,8 +233,8 @@ When `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) and the tie
 
 1. Check `git` availability (`git --version`). `git` is effectively guaranteed at Deep tier (via `gh` dependency) but NOT guaranteed at Forge tier.
 2. If `git` is available: perform an ephemeral shallow clone to a system temp path (`{system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/`).
-3. For create-skill: use `--depth 1 --single-branch --filter=blob:none`; apply sparse-checkout if `include_patterns` are specified.
-4. For update-skill: use sparse-checkout scoped to the changed files from the change manifest only. No `--branch` flag — uses the remote default branch (must match the branch used during original create-skill run).
+3. For create-skill: use `--depth 1 --single-branch --filter=blob:none`; if `include_patterns` are specified, convert glob patterns to directory roots before passing to `sparse-checkout set` — `git sparse-checkout` expects directories, not globs. Use `--skip-checks` when any individual file paths (no glob characters) are present. Apply original glob patterns as file-level filters after checkout. See step-03-extract.md for the full conversion rules.
+4. For update-skill: use sparse-checkout with `--skip-checks` scoped to the changed files from the change manifest only (file paths require `--skip-checks`). No `--branch` flag — uses the remote default branch (must match the branch used during original create-skill run).
 5. If clone succeeds: use the local clone path for AST extraction. All results are T1 with `[AST:...]` citations.
 6. Cleanup: delete the temp directory after extraction inventory is built and all data is in context. The clone never persists beyond the extraction step.
 
diff --git a/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md b/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md
index bf57134..ae436a7 100644
--- a/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md
+++ b/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md
@@ -93,13 +93,50 @@ If `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is F
    git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
    ```
 
-   If `include_patterns` are specified in the brief, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:
+   **If `include_patterns` are NOT specified:**
+
+   ```
+   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
+   ```
+
+   **If `include_patterns` ARE specified**, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:
 
    ```
    git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}
-   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {include_patterns}
    ```
 
+   **IMPORTANT:** `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns. Convert `include_patterns` before passing them:
+
+   **Classification rule:** A pattern is an **individual file** if it contains no glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) AND does not end with `/`. Everything else is a glob — strip it to its directory root (the path prefix before the first glob character or wildcard segment).
+
+   - Strip glob suffixes to directory roots (e.g., `src/core/**/*.py` → `src/core`, `src/api/*.ts` → `src/api`)
+   - Deduplicate the resulting directory list
+   - Individual files (e.g., `pyproject.toml`, `src/utils/helpers.py`) are kept as-is
+
+   **If only directory roots (no individual files):**
+
+   ```
+   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {converted_directory_roots}
+   ```
+
+   **If any individual files are present (or mixed):**
+
+   ```
+   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}
+   ```
+
+   Example transformation:
+   ```
+   Brief include_patterns:        sparse-checkout args:
+   src/core/**/*.py          →    src/core          (directory root)
+   src/api/*.ts              →    src/api           (directory root)
+   examples/**/*.py          →    examples          (directory root)
+   pyproject.toml            →    pyproject.toml    (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
+   src/utils/helpers.py      →    src/utils/helpers.py (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
+   ```
+
+   After checkout, apply the original glob `include_patterns` as file-level filters when building the extraction file list — sparse-checkout gets the right directories, glob filtering narrows to the exact files.
+
 3. **If clone succeeds:** Update the working source path to `{temp_path}` for all subsequent AST operations in this step. Proceed with the **Forge/Deep Tier** extraction strategy below. Mark `ephemeral_clone_active = true` for cleanup.
 
 4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**

title:	gh release list --json body is not supported — temporal fetch fails
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	31
--
## Step
`step-03b-fetch-temporal.md` — Section 3, Releases fetch

## Problem
The template command in the workflow:

```bash
gh release list -R {owner}/{repo} --limit 10 --json tagName,name,publishedAt,body | ...
```

fails because `gh release list` does **not** support the `body` field in its `--json` output. The command returns empty/invalid JSON, causing the Python parser to fail with `JSONDecodeError`.

The `body` field is only available via `gh release view {tag}`, not `gh release list`.

## Workaround Used
Iterated over release tags from `gh release list` (tabular output), then fetched each release individually with `gh release view {tag}`.

## Suggested Fix
Update the releases fetch template to either:

1. **Two-step approach** (recommended):
```bash
# Get tag list
gh release list -R {owner}/{repo} --limit 10 | awk '{print $1}'
# Then for each tag:
gh release view {tag} -R {owner}/{repo}
```

2. Or document the fallback explicitly in the step file.

## Impact
**Execution failure** — releases fetch fails silently (empty JSON), losing release context unless workaround applied.
```

     6→
     7→# Step 3b: Fetch Temporal Context
     8→
     9→## STEP GOAL:
    10→
    11→To fetch temporal context (issues, PRs, changelogs, release notes) from the source repository and index it into a QMD collection for Deep tier enrichment. This ensures step-04 has historical data to search when annotating extracted functions with T2 provenance.
    12→
    13→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    14→
    15→### Universal Rules:
    16→
    17→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    18→- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file
    19→- ⚙️ TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread
    20→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
    21→
    22→### Role Reinforcement:
    23→
    24→- ✅ You are a skill compilation engine fetching temporal data for QMD enrichment
    25→- ✅ Temporal context is best-effort — failures never block the workflow
    26→- ✅ Graceful degradation is paramount — non-GitHub repos or missing tools are silent skips, not errors
    27→
    28→### Step-Specific Rules:
    29→
    30→- 🎯 Deep tier only — Quick and Forge tiers skip this step entirely and silently
    31→- 🎯 GitHub repositories only — other source types degrade gracefully
    32→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to halt the workflow if fetching or indexing fails
    33→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to modify extraction data from step-03 — this step only creates QMD collections
    34→- 💾 Write fetched content to a staging directory, index into QMD, then clean up
    35→
    36→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    37→
    38→- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    39→- 💾 Write fetched markdown files to a staging directory: `_bmad-output/{skill-name}-temporal/`
    40→- 📖 Index the staging directory into QMD and register in `forge-tier.yaml`
    41→- 🚫 Never leave staging files on disk after indexing — always clean up
    42→
    43→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    44→
    45→- Available: brief_data, tier, source_location from step-01; extraction_inventory from step-03
    46→- **Used from extraction_inventory:** `top_exports[]` — the list of top-level public API function names (typically 10-20). Used for targeted GitHub searches (section 3b).
    47→- Focus: Creating a QMD temporal collection for the source repository
    48→- Limits: Do NOT modify extraction data, begin enrichment, or compile content
    49→- Dependencies: Extraction must be complete from step-03
    50→
    51→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    52→
    53→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    54→
    55→### 1. Check Eligibility
    56→
    57→Evaluate the following conditions sequentially. **If ANY condition fails, skip silently to section 5 (auto-proceed) with no output:**
    58→
    59→1. **Tier is Deep:** If tier is Quick or Forge, skip silently.
    60→2. **Source is GitHub:** Verify `source_repo` is a GitHub URL (`https://github.com/...`) or `owner/repo` format. If the source is a local path, a non-GitHub URL, or any other format, skip silently.
    61→3. **`gh` CLI is available:** Run `gh auth status` to verify the CLI is installed and authenticated. If it fails, skip silently.
    62→
    63→All three conditions must pass to proceed to section 2.
    64→
    65→### 2. Check Cache (Skip If Fresh)
    66→
    67→Read `forge-tier.yaml` from the sidecar path.
    68→
    69→- Look for a `qmd_collections` entry where `skill_name` matches the current brief AND `type` is `"temporal"`.
    70→- If found AND `created_at` is within the last **7 days**: the temporal collection is fresh. Display:
    71→
    72→"**Temporal context: cached.** Collection `{skill-name}-temporal` is fresh ({days} days old). Skipping re-fetch."
    73→
    74→Skip to section 5 (auto-proceed).
    75→
    76→- If not found OR `created_at` is older than 7 days: continue to section 3.
    77→
    78→### 3. Fetch Temporal Context
    79→
    80→Create a staging directory: `_bmad-output/{skill-name}-temporal/`
    81→
    82→Resolve the `owner` and `repo` from `source_repo` (e.g., `topoteretes/cognee` from `https://github.com/topoteretes/cognee`).
    83→
    84→Execute the following fetches, writing output as markdown files to the staging directory. **If any individual fetch fails, log a warning and continue with the others:**
    85→
    86→1. **Issues (last 100):**
    87→
    88→   ```bash
    89→   gh issue list -R {owner}/{repo} --state all --limit 100 --json number,title,state,labels,createdAt,closedAt,body | ...
    90→   ```
    91→
    92→   Write to `{staging}/issues.md` — format as a markdown document with one section per issue (number, title, state, labels, body summary).
    93→
    94→2. **Merged PRs (last 100):**
    95→
    96→   ```bash
    97→   gh pr list -R {owner}/{repo} --state merged --limit 100 --json number,title,mergedAt,labels,body | ...
    98→   ```
    99→
   100→   Write to `{staging}/prs.md` — format as a markdown document with one section per PR.
   101→
   102→3. **Releases (last 10):**
   103→
   104→   ```bash
   105→   gh release list -R {owner}/{repo} --limit 10 --json tagName,name,publishedAt,body | ...
   106→   ```
   107→
   108→   Write to `{staging}/releases.md` — format as a markdown document with one section per release.
   109→
   110→4. **Changelog (if exists):**
   111→
   112→   Check if `CHANGELOG.md` or `RELEASES.md` exists in the repository root:
   113→
   114→   ```bash
   115→   gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/CHANGELOG.md --jq '.content' | base64 -d
   116→   ```
   117→
   118→   If found, write to `{staging}/changelog.md`. If not found (404), skip silently.
   119→
   120→#### 3b. Targeted Function Searches (Uses Extraction Inventory)
   121→
   122→After the generic fetches above, perform **targeted searches** using the top-level public API function names from `extraction_inventory.top_exports[]`. This produces high-signal results that generic list fetches miss.
   123→
   124→**Limit:** Search the top **10 function names** maximum to control API call volume and avoid `gh` rate limiting.
   125→
   126→For each function name in `top_exports[]` (up to 10):
   127→
   128→```bash
   129→gh search issues --repo {owner}/{repo} "{function_name}" --limit 5 --json number,title,state,body
   130→```
   131→
   132→Aggregate all targeted search results into a single file: `{staging}/targeted-issues.md`. Format as a markdown document with one section per function name, listing the matching issues/PRs found.
   133→
   134→**If `gh search` is unavailable** (older `gh` CLI versions): skip targeted searches silently. The generic fetches from section 3 still provide baseline temporal context.
   135→
   136→**If rate limiting occurs** (HTTP 429 or similar): stop targeted searches immediately, keep results collected so far. Log: "Targeted search stopped at function {N}/{total} due to rate limiting."
   137→
   138→**After all fetching,** verify at least one file was written to the staging directory. If the staging directory is empty (all fetches failed), log a warning and skip to section 5.
   139→
   140→### 4. Index Into QMD & Register
   141→
   142→**Index the staging directory:**
   143→
   144→If a `{skill-name}-temporal` collection already exists, remove and recreate for atomic replace:
   145→
   146→```bash
   147→qmd collection remove {skill-name}-temporal
   148→qmd collection add _bmad-output/{skill-name}-temporal/ --name {skill-name}-temporal --mask "*.md"
   149→```
   150→
   151→**Update the registry** in `forge-tier.yaml`:
   152→
   153→If an entry with `name: "{skill-name}-temporal"` already exists in `qmd_collections`, replace it. Otherwise, append:
   154→
   155→```yaml
   156→  - name: "{skill-name}-temporal"
   157→    type: "temporal"
   158→    source_workflow: "create-skill"
   159→    skill_name: "{skill-name}"
   160→    created_at: "{current ISO date}"
   161→```
   162→
   163→**Clean up** the staging directory after successful indexing:
   164→
   165→```bash
   166→rm -rf _bmad-output/{skill-name}-temporal/
   167→```
   168→
   169→**Error handling:**
   170→
   171→- If QMD indexing fails: log the error, note that temporal enrichment will be unavailable. Do NOT fail the workflow.
   172→- If registry update fails: log the error, continue. The collection may exist in QMD even if the registry entry failed.
   173→- If cleanup fails: log a warning and continue.
   174→
   175→Display brief confirmation:
   176→
   177→"**Temporal context indexed.** Collection `{skill-name}-temporal` created ({file_count} files: {list files}). Proceeding to enrichment..."
   178→
   179→### 5. Menu Handling Logic
   180→
   181→**Auto-proceed step — no user interaction.**
   182→
   183→After temporal context is fetched and indexed (or skipped for any reason), immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
   184→
   185→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   186→
   187→- This is an auto-proceed step with no user choices
   188→- Quick/Forge tiers skip directly to next step with no output
   189→- Non-GitHub sources skip directly to next step with no output
   190→- Cached collections (< 7 days old) skip with brief cache-hit message
   191→- Deep tier with fresh fetch displays brief confirmation then auto-proceeds
   192→- All failures degrade gracefully — skip and auto-proceed
   193→
   194→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   195→
   196→ONLY WHEN temporal context is indexed into QMD (or the step is skipped due to eligibility, cache, or failure) will you proceed to load `{nextStepFile}` for QMD enrichment.
   197→
   198→---
   199→
   200→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   201→
   202→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   203→
   204→- Non-eligible scenarios (Quick/Forge tier, non-GitHub source, no `gh` CLI) skipped silently
   205→- Cached collections (< 7 days old) detected and re-fetch skipped
   206→- Temporal data fetched via `gh` CLI into staging directory (generic + targeted)
   207→- Targeted searches performed for up to 10 top_exports function names
   208→- At least one temporal file written (issues, PRs, releases, changelog, or targeted-issues)
   209→- Collection `{skill-name}-temporal` indexed into QMD
   210→- Registry entry added/updated in forge-tier.yaml with type `"temporal"`
   211→- Staging directory cleaned up after indexing
   212→- Auto-proceeded to step-04
   213→
   214→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   215→
   216→- Halting the workflow due to a `gh` CLI, QMD, or network failure
   217→- Leaving staging files on disk after indexing (must clean up)
   218→- Overwriting or modifying extraction data from step-03
   219→- Displaying skip messages for Quick/Forge tiers (should be silent)
   220→- Attempting to fetch temporal data from non-GitHub sources
   221→- Not registering the collection in forge-tier.yaml after successful indexing
   222→
   223→**Master Rule:** Temporal context is best-effort enrichment data. Fetch what you can, index it, clean up, and move on. Failures degrade gracefully — they never block the skill compilation pipeline.

I now have a thorough understanding of the change, the full codebase context, and all the specific issues to report. Here is the complete review.

---

## Review: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`

The change replaces the single `gh release list --json tagName,name,publishedAt,body` call with a two-step approach because `body` is not a supported JSON field for `gh release list`. The bug fix is correct in substance. However, the implementation of the fix introduces several issues of varying severity.

---

## Critical Issues

### 1. `gh release view` output is human-readable text, not JSON — the agent has no structured way to extract `name`, `publishedAt`, or `tagName` from it

**Confidence: 95**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`, lines 110-114

`gh release view {tag} -R {owner}/{repo}` with no flags returns a human-readable text block (title, tag, draft state, prerelease flag, release notes). It does not output JSON. However, the instruction at line 114 says to write a document with `tag, name, date, body` — where `name` and `date` were retrieved in Step 1, and the body must be parsed from the human-readable output of Step 2.

The problem is that the code block shows both commands together without specifying that the agent needs to (a) parse the JSON from Step 1 to get `tagName`, `name`, and `publishedAt` for each tag, (b) iterate and run `gh release view` for each tag to get the body text, then (c) correlate both results to write a unified section. An AI agent following this literally could:

- Run Step 1 once (gets a JSON array of 10 releases).
- Run Step 2 once with a literal `{tag}` placeholder (or just the first tag).
- Not iterate at all, since the instruction says "for each tag" only in prose but shows both commands in a single static block.

`gh release view` does support `--json body,tagName,name,publishedAt` — that flag combination IS valid on `gh release view` even though it is not valid on `gh release list`. The fix could use structured output for the per-release call:

```bash
# Step 1: Get release tags
gh release list -R {owner}/{repo} --limit 10 --json tagName,name,publishedAt

# Step 2: For each tagName from Step 1, fetch the release body
gh release view {tagName} -R {owner}/{repo} --json tagName,name,publishedAt,body
```

This makes Step 2's output machine-parseable and removes any ambiguity about how to merge data from both steps. The current form forces the agent to scrape unstructured text.

---

### 2. No explicit iteration instruction and no error-handling spec for the per-tag loop

**Confidence: 88**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`, lines 104-114

The two-step approach requires a loop — "for each tag from Step 1, run `gh release view` against that tag". This is never stated explicitly. The existing error-handling pattern for all other fetches in this file is: "If any individual fetch fails, log a warning and continue with the others" (line 84). There is no equivalent for the per-tag `gh release view` calls.

An agent that takes `gh release view` literally as a single command (with `{tag}` as an unresolved template variable) will produce either a shell error or an empty result for all 10 releases, and the step-level graceful-degradation rules will treat the entire releases section as a failure — correctly, but silently, discarding all release data with no diagnostic.

The step should state explicitly:

> For each `tagName` returned by Step 1, run `gh release view {tagName} -R {owner}/{repo} --json tagName,name,publishedAt,body`. If a specific release view fails, log a warning and skip that release. Continue with remaining tags.

---

## Important Issues

### 3. Zero-release edge case is not handled for the two-step approach specifically

**Confidence: 85**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`, lines 106-114

The general empty-staging-directory guard exists at line 144, but there is no guidance specific to the releases fetch for the case where `gh release list` returns zero results (a very common case for newer or non-versioned repos). With the original single-command approach, zero results would produce an empty JSON array and the agent would trivially write an empty section or nothing. With the two-step approach, Step 1 returning an empty array means Step 2 should not be run at all. This is not stated. An agent could attempt `gh release view` with an empty tag string, producing an error that bubbles up and gets mis-categorized.

The fix should add: "If Step 1 returns an empty array, skip Step 2 and omit the releases section entirely."

### 4. Rate-limiting risk for the per-release calls is not acknowledged in the rate-limiting section

**Confidence: 82**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`, line 142

The rate-limiting guidance in section 3b (line 142) explicitly says "stop targeted searches immediately" when an HTTP 429 is received. The new per-release loop in section 3 (items 1-4) has no equivalent rate-limiting guard. With the original single-command approach, releases were one API call. The two-step approach now makes up to 11 API calls just for releases (1 list + up to 10 view calls). This is in addition to the issues fetch, PRs fetch, changelog fetch, and up to 10 targeted searches — a maximum of ~122 API calls total in the worst case.

The rate-limiting note in section 3b covers only `gh search`. The new release loop should include: "If `gh release view` returns HTTP 429, stop the release loop, keep results collected so far, and log a warning."

---

## Scope Check: Files That Need Corresponding Updates

The `gh release` fix is **isolated to one file**. The search confirms:

- `update-skill` has no temporal fetch step and no `gh release` references. No change needed.
- `create-stack-skill` has no temporal fetch step. No change needed.
- `workflow-plan-create-skill.md` references `gh_bridge` for "releases, changelog" at line 202 but this is a capability description, not a command — no change needed.
- No other `.md` file anywhere in `src/` contains `gh release list` or `gh release view`.

The fix does not need to propagate to any other file.

---

## Summary

The core bug fix (removing `body` from `gh release list --json`) is correct — `body` is not supported there. The fix introduces no breaking changes to the broader workflow. However, the revised instruction block has three compounding problems:

1. `gh release view` should use `--json tagName,name,publishedAt,body` (which IS supported) to give the agent structured output rather than human-readable text.
2. The iteration over tags must be made explicit with its own per-item failure handling.
3. Zero-tag and rate-limit edge cases for the new loop are unspecified.

The key fix is at line 111 of `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`: change `gh release view {tag} -R {owner}/{repo}` to `gh release view {tagName} -R {owner}/{repo} --json tagName,name,publishedAt,body`, and add prose that explicitly iterates over each tag from Step 1's JSON array with per-item error handling and a zero-results early exit.

title:	Brief version vs source version mismatch — no validation or reconciliation guidance
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	32
--
## Step
`step-01-load-brief.md` — Section 3 (Validate Brief Structure) or `step-03-extract.md`

## Problem
The brief's `version` field may not match the actual source version. In the cognee skill build:

- **Brief:** `version: "1.0.0"` (set during brief-skill workflow)
- **Actual source (pyproject.toml):** `version: "0.5.5"`

The workflow has no validation rule for this mismatch, no guidance on which takes precedence, and no warning mechanism. The compilation engine must silently decide which version to use.

## Workaround Used
Used the actual source version (`0.5.5`) and noted the discrepancy in the evidence report warnings.

## Suggested Fix
Add a version reconciliation step, either in step-01 (after brief validation) or step-03 (after source resolution):

1. If source is accessible, extract actual version from `pyproject.toml`, `package.json`, `Cargo.toml`, etc.
2. Compare against `brief.version`
3. If mismatch:
   - Warn: "Brief version ({brief}) differs from source version ({actual}). Using source version."
   - Update the working version in context to the source version
4. Record the mismatch in evidence report

Alternatively, the brief-skill workflow could auto-detect the version during scope configuration rather than asking the user to set it manually.

## Impact
**Data accuracy** — skill metadata may report wrong version if the brief version is used uncritically.
```

    51→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    52→
    53→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    54→
    55→### 1. Load Extraction Patterns
    56→
    57→Load `{extractionPatternsData}` completely. Identify the strategy for the current forge tier.
    58→
    59→### 2. Apply Scope Filters
    60→
    61→From the brief, apply scope and pattern filters:
    62→
    63→- `scope` — determines what to extract (e.g., "all public exports", specific modules)
    64→- `include_patterns` — file globs to include (if specified)
    65→- `exclude_patterns` — file globs to exclude (if specified)
    66→
    67→Build the filtered file list from the source tree resolved in step-01.
    68→
    69→### 3. Check for Docs-Only Mode
    70→
    71→**If `source_type: "docs-only"` in the brief data:**
    72→
    73→"**Docs-only mode:** No source code to extract. Documentation content will be fetched from `doc_urls` in step-03c."
    74→
    75→Build an empty extraction inventory with zero exports. Set `extraction_mode: "docs-only"` in context. Auto-proceed through Gate 2 (section 5) — display the empty inventory and note that T3 content will be produced by the doc-fetcher step.
    76→
    77→**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue with extraction below.
    78→
    79→### 4. Execute Tier-Dependent Extraction
    80→
    81→**Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep only):**
    82→
    83→If `source_repo` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
    84→
    85→If `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:
    86→
    87→1. **Check `git` availability:** Verify `git` is functional (`git --version`). If `git` is not available, skip to the fallback warning below.
    88→
    89→2. **Ephemeral shallow clone:** Clone the repository to a system temp path for AST access:
    90→
    91→   ```
    92→   temp_path = {system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/
    93→   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
    94→   ```
    95→
    96→   **If `include_patterns` are NOT specified:**
    97→
    98→   ```
    99→   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
   100→   ```
   101→
   102→   **If `include_patterns` ARE specified**, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:
   103→
   104→   ```
   105→   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}
   106→   ```
   107→
   108→   **IMPORTANT:** `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns. Convert `include_patterns` before passing them:
   109→
   110→   **Classification rule:** A pattern is an **individual file** if it contains no glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) AND does not end with `/`. Everything else is a glob — strip it to its directory root (the path prefix before the first glob character or wildcard segment).
   111→
   112→   - Strip glob suffixes to directory roots (e.g., `src/core/**/*.py` → `src/core`, `src/api/*.ts` → `src/api`)
   113→   - Deduplicate the resulting directory list
   114→   - Individual files (e.g., `pyproject.toml`, `src/utils/helpers.py`) are kept as-is
   115→
   116→   **If only directory roots (no individual files):**
   117→
   118→   ```
   119→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {converted_directory_roots}
   120→   ```
   121→
   122→   **If any individual files are present (or mixed):**
   123→
   124→   ```
   125→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}
   126→   ```
   127→
   128→   Example transformation:
   129→   ```
   130→   Brief include_patterns:        sparse-checkout args:
   131→   src/core/**/*.py          →    src/core          (directory root)
   132→   src/api/*.ts              →    src/api           (directory root)
   133→   examples/**/*.py          →    examples          (directory root)
   134→   pyproject.toml            →    pyproject.toml    (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
   135→   src/utils/helpers.py      →    src/utils/helpers.py (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
   136→   ```
   137→
   138→   After checkout, apply the original glob `include_patterns` as file-level filters when building the extraction file list — sparse-checkout gets the right directories, glob filtering narrows to the exact files.
```

   141→
   142→4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**

   145→
   146→   "Ephemeral clone of `{source_repo}` failed: {error}. Degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and update `source_repo` in your brief to the local path."
   147→
   148→   Proceed with Quick tier extraction strategy below. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
   149→
   150→**Ephemeral clone cleanup:** After extraction is complete for all files in scope (whether successful or partially failed), before presenting the Gate 2 summary (Section 6), if `ephemeral_clone_active`, delete the `{temp_path}` directory. Log: "Ephemeral source clone cleaned up." This ensures cleanup runs even if some extractions failed, as long as the step itself is still executing.
   151→
   152→**Quick Tier (No AST tools):**
   153→
   154→1. Use `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` to map source structure (if remote)
   155→2. Identify entry points: index files, main exports, public modules
   156→3. Use `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, path)` to read each entry point
   157→4. Extract from source text: exported function names, parameter lists, return types
   158→5. Infer types from JSDoc, docstrings, type annotations
   159→6. Confidence: All results T1-low — `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]`
   160→
   161→**Forge/Deep Tier (AST available):**
   162→
   163→⚠️ **CRITICAL:** Before executing AST extraction, load the **AST Extraction Protocol** section from `{extractionPatternsData}`. Follow the decision tree based on the file count from step-01's file tree. This determines whether to use the MCP tool, scoped YAML rules, or CLI streaming. Never use `ast-grep --json` (without `=stream`) — it loads the entire result set into memory and will fail on large codebases.
   164→
   165→1. Detect language from brief or file extensions
   166→2. Follow the AST Extraction Protocol decision tree from `{extractionPatternsData}`:
   167→   - ≤100 files: use `find_code()` MCP tool with `max_results` and `output_format="text"`
   168→   - ≤500 files: use `find_code_by_rule()` MCP tool with scoped YAML rules
   169→   - >500 files: use CLI `--json=stream` with line-by-line streaming Python
   170→3. For each export: extract function name, full signature, parameter types, return type, line number
   171→4. Use `ast_bridge.detect_co_imports(path, libraries[])` to find integration points
   172→5. Build extraction rules YAML data for reproducibility
   173→6. Confidence: All results T1 — `[AST:{file}:L{line}]`
   174→
   175→**If AST tool is unavailable at Forge/Deep tier:**
   176→
   177→⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:** "AST tools are unavailable — extraction will use source reading (T1-low). Run [SF] Setup Forge to detect and configure AST tools for T1 confidence."

     1→# Skill Brief Schema
     2→
     3→## Required Fields
     4→
     5→| Field | Type | Description | Validation |
     6→|-------|------|-------------|------------|
     7→| name | string | Kebab-case skill name | Must match `[a-z0-9-]+` |
     8→| version | string | Semantic version | Must match `X.Y.Z` pattern, default "1.0.0" |
     9→| source_repo | string | GitHub URL or local path | Must be accessible |
    10→| language | string | Primary programming language | Must be detected or user-specified |
    11→| scope | object | Inclusion/exclusion boundaries | Must have type + at least one pattern |
    12→| description | string | What this skill covers | 1-3 sentences |
    13→| forge_tier | string | Compilation tier | One of: quick, forge, deep |
    14→| created | string | Creation date | ISO date format |
    15→| created_by | string | User who created the brief | From config user_name |
    16→
    17→## Optional Fields
    18→
    19→| Field | Type | Description | Validation |
    20→|-------|------|-------------|------------|
    21→| source_type | string | Source mode: `source` or `docs-only` | Default `source`. When `docs-only`: `source_repo` optional, `doc_urls` required |
    22→| doc_urls | array | Documentation URLs for T3 content | Each entry: `{url, label}`. Required when `source_type: "docs-only"` |
    23→
    24→When `source_type: "docs-only"`:
    25→- `source_repo` becomes optional (set to doc site URL for reference)
    26→- `doc_urls` must have at least one entry
    27→- `source_authority` is forced to `community` (T3 external documentation cannot be `official`)
    28→- All extracted content gets `[EXT:{url}]` citations
    29→
    30→## Scope Object Structure
    31→
    32→```yaml
    33→scope:
    34→  type: full-library | specific-modules | public-api
    35→  include:
    36→    - "src/**/*.ts"           # Glob patterns for included files/directories
    37→  exclude:
    38→    - "**/*.test.*"           # Glob patterns for excluded files
    39→    - "**/node_modules/**"
    40→  notes: "Optional notes about scope decisions"
    41→```
    42→
    43→## YAML Template
    44→
    45→```yaml
    46→---
    47→name: "{skill-name}"
    48→version: "1.0.0"
    49→source_type: "source"                    # "source" (default) or "docs-only"
    50→source_repo: "{github-url-or-local-path}"
    51→language: "{detected-language}"
    52→description: "{brief-description}"
    53→forge_tier: "{quick|forge|deep}"
    54→created: "{date}"
    55→created_by: "{user_name}"
    56→scope:
    57→  type: "{full-library|specific-modules|public-api|docs-only}"
    58→  include:
    59→    - "{pattern}"
    60→  exclude:
    61→    - "{pattern}"
    62→  notes: "{optional-scope-notes}"
    63→# Optional: documentation URLs for T3 content (required when source_type: "docs-only")
    64→# doc_urls:
    65→#   - url: "https://docs.example.com/api"
    66→#     label: "API Reference"
    67→---
    68→```
    69→
    70→## Human-Readable Presentation Format
    71→
    72→When presenting the brief for confirmation (step 04), display as:
    73→
    74→```
    75→Skill Brief: {name}
    76→====================
    77→
    78→Target:      {source_repo}
    79→Language:    {language}
    80→Forge Tier:  {forge_tier}
