I now have all the information needed for a complete, confident review. Here is the full analysis.

---

## Review: Issue #32 — Version Reconciliation Changes Across 4 Files

Reviewing 4 changed files against the broader `src/` directory for correctness, consistency, coverage, and edge-case handling.

---

## Critical Issues

### 1. Version Reconciliation Fires Before the Ephemeral Clone Exists

Confidence: **95**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`, lines 152–169

The new "Version Reconciliation" subsection instructs the agent to check version files "Before beginning extraction" and to look in "the resolved source path (local or ephemeral clone)". However, the ephemeral clone is created inside section 4's "Remote Source Resolution" block (lines 86–148), which runs *after* the reconciliation instruction at lines 152–169.

For a remote repo at Forge/Deep tier the execution order is:
1. Section 3 — Docs-only mode check
2. Section 4 — Remote Source Resolution (clone happens here, `temp_path` is set here)
3. **Version Reconciliation** — but the instruction says to look in "the resolved source path (local or ephemeral clone)"

Because the reconciliation block is placed *after* the clone code in the file but its wording says "before beginning extraction", an AI agent following the file top-to-bottom could reasonably either: (a) try to read version files before the clone exists — i.e., from a non-existent `temp_path` — or (b) correctly infer the intent and run it post-clone. The positioning is ambiguous and the word "before" directly contradicts the file order.

The block also has a special case "If source is remote and accessed via Quick tier (gh_bridge, no local files)" at line 169, which covers the Quick-tier remote path, but the Forge/Deep clone path isn't explicitly sequenced.

Fix: Move the "Version Reconciliation" subsection to immediately after the ephemeral clone cleanup paragraph (line 150) but before "Quick Tier" instructions begin (line 171), and remove the word "Before beginning extraction" from the opening. Replace with a clearer sequencing statement, such as: "After the ephemeral clone is available (or local source is confirmed), and before proceeding with API extraction:".

---

### 2. Docs-Only Mode: Version Reconciliation Runs but Is Meaningless

Confidence: **90**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`, line 152

The reconciliation header says "(all tiers, all source types)". Section 3 of the same file (lines 69–77) handles docs-only mode: it sets `extraction_mode: "docs-only"` and explicitly auto-proceeds through Gate 2, bypassing the rest of section 4. But because the reconciliation block is textually positioned inside section 4 and marked "all source types", an agent reading the file may try to look for `pyproject.toml`, `package.json`, etc. in a skill that has no source repo at all — only `doc_urls`.

For docs-only mode, the brief schema (`skill-brief-schema.md` line 8) correctly specifies that `source_repo` becomes optional. The version field is not meaningful for docs-only skills, and there are no version files to check.

The reconciliation block provides no guard clause for `source_type: "docs-only"`. The Step 3 early-exit ("Auto-proceed through Gate 2") would skip over it in practice, but only if the agent correctly recognizes the early-exit means leaving all of section 4. That is a fragile assumption — a different agent could complete section 3 and then continue reading and executing section 4 including reconciliation.

Fix: Add a guard at the top of the Version Reconciliation subsection: "If `source_type: "docs-only"`: skip this subsection. No version files exist to reconcile."

---

### 3. `analyze-source` workflow hardcodes `version: '1.0.0'` — Not Updated

Confidence: **88**

Files:
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md`, lines 12 and 52
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/analyze-source/steps-c/step-06-generate-briefs.md`, line 81

The `brief-skill` schema was updated with auto-detection rules and a dynamic template. However, `analyze-source` uses its own copy of the schema (`src/workflows/analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md`) which still shows `version: '1.0.0'` as a fixed default in both the table (line 12) and the YAML template (line 52). The `step-06-generate-briefs.md` field mapping table (line 81) explicitly instructs: `version | '1.0.0' (default for new briefs)`.

This means any briefs generated by `analyze-source` will always carry `version: '1.0.0'` even when the source has a detectable version. Those briefs then flow into `create-skill`, where the reconciliation in step-03-extract will fire and warn/override — which is the correct ultimate behavior — but the user never gets the earlier, cleaner reconciliation that `brief-skill` now provides. More importantly, the two schema files describe different behavior for the same field, creating confusion about what "the schema" requires.

Fix: Apply the same "Version Detection" section and dynamic YAML template to `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md`, and update `step-06-generate-briefs.md` step 2's field mapping to say `version | Auto-detect from source (see schema), fall back to '1.0.0'`.

---

### 4. `create-stack-skill` metadata.json hardcodes `"version": "1.0.0"` With No Reconciliation

Confidence: **85**

Files:
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md`, line 117
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/data/stack-skill-template.md`, line 67

`create-stack-skill` never participates in the `brief-skill → create-skill` path, so it skips both the brief-time detection and the step-03-extract reconciliation entirely. The metadata.json template for stack skills hardcodes `"version": "1.0.0"` unconditionally. A stack skill wrapping, say, React 18.3.1 and React Router 6.2.0 will always report `"version": "1.0.0"`, regardless of what the component libraries' manifests say.

The per-library reference files in `step-07-generate-output.md` section 3 do state "Library name, version from manifest", so the individual library versions flow into the reference `.md` files — but the top-level `metadata.json.version` field, which `update-skill/step-01-init.md` reads back to display in the baseline summary, stays perpetually wrong.

This was a pre-existing issue but the PR's version detection logic is explicitly scoped to "all tiers, all source types" in step-03-extract, yet `create-stack-skill` has its own parallel path that the PR did not address.

Fix: Add a note in the PR scope or, better, update `create-stack-skill/step-07-generate-output.md` section 6 to derive `version` from the primary library's detected version (the one with the highest import count or the one matching the project's own manifest), falling back to `"1.0.0"`.

---

## Important Issues

### 5. `quick-skill` metadata.json hardcodes `"version": "0.1.0"` — Inconsistent Default

Confidence: **82**

Files:
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md`, line 120
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md`, line 67

`quick-skill/step-03-quick-extract.md` section 2 already extracts the `version` field from the package manifest (it's listed in the `extraction_inventory` structure at line 117). The subsequent compile step (`step-04-compile.md`, line 120) then ignores that extracted version and hardcodes `"0.1.0"` in the metadata.json template. This means the version present in `extraction_inventory.version` is never propagated to the output artifact.

The PR introduced per-source-type reconciliation in `create-skill` but did not address the `quick-skill` path, which already had the version data available and was discarding it.

Fix: In `step-04-compile.md` section 4 (Generate Metadata JSON), replace `"version": "0.1.0"` with `"version": "{extraction_inventory.version or '0.1.0'}"`.

---

### 6. `update-skill` version bump instruction doesn't account for source version override

Confidence: **80**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md`, line 67

`update-skill` step-06-write section 2 says: "Update `version` (increment patch version)." This is a mechanical patch-bump (e.g., `1.2.3 → 1.2.4`). But if the source library has jumped from `1.2.3` to `2.0.0`, a patch bump produces a metadata.json that says `v1.2.4` while the skill now documents `v2.0.0` source. The `create-skill` step-03-extract's reconciliation logic runs at creation time, but `update-skill` has its own re-extraction step (`step-03-re-extract.md`) with no equivalent version reconciliation block.

The new PR adds reconciliation only to `create-skill/step-03-extract.md`. The `update-skill` pipeline (`step-03-re-extract.md`) follows a different code path that accesses the same manifest files during ephemeral clone, but has no instruction to re-check the source version.

Fix: Add a version reconciliation check to `update-skill/step-03-re-extract.md` (after the ephemeral clone cleanup, mirroring the pattern from `create-skill/step-03-extract.md`). The metadata.json write in step-06 should set `version` to the source-detected version if one was found, rather than always incrementing the patch.

---

## Confirmed Correct Behaviors

The following concerns raised in the review focus were verified to be handled correctly:

**Existing briefs with `version: "1.0.0"` that match source**: The reconciliation check at step-03-extract line 161 only warns and overrides when versions *differ*. If source also has `1.0.0`, no warning fires and no change occurs. This correctly handles the common new-project case.

**No version file found**: Line 167 explicitly states: "If no version file is found or version cannot be extracted: keep `brief.version` as-is. No warning needed." This is correct.

**Non-semver source version**: The `brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md` line 41 states: "If it fails or returns a non-semver value, fall back to `'1.0.0'`." However, step-03-extract has no equivalent guard — if a source file contains a non-semver version (e.g., a Python `version = "2023.1.dev0"`) it will be used as-is and overwrite a valid `brief.version`. This is a minor gap but falls below the 80-confidence threshold for reporting as a standalone bug, since the brief-skill schema does document the fallback and an AI agent may apply that pattern generally.

**Remote Quick-tier path**: Line 169 explicitly covers this: `gh_bridge.read_file` attempt with graceful fallback. Handled correctly.

**`step-05-compile.md` and `step-08-report.md` version consumption**: Both files consume `{version}` from context (set by step-01 from `brief.version`, then potentially overridden by step-03's reconciliation). The override at line 164 ("Update the working version in context to the source version") correctly flows into both the compile step's Section 1 Overview and the report step's banner at line 54. The metadata.json structure in `skill-sections.md` uses `"{source-version}"` as the value, which correctly receives whatever is in the working context version. No issues here.

**Monorepo with multiple version files**: The instruction "Look for the first matching file" (line 154) defines a deterministic lookup order per language. This is intentionally first-match wins, which is reasonable for monorepos — the agent picks the root manifest. This is acceptable behavior, though a note acknowledging the limitation for monorepos with per-package version files would improve clarity.

---

## Summary Table

| # | Severity | File | Confidence |
|---|----------|------|------------|
| 1 | Critical | `create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` L152 | 95 |
| 2 | Critical | `create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` L152 | 90 |
| 3 | Critical | `analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md` + `step-06-generate-briefs.md` | 88 |
| 4 | Critical | `create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md` L117 | 85 |
| 5 | Important | `quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md` L120 | 82 |
| 6 | Important | `update-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` L67 | 80 |

The core logic of the PR is sound — the reconciliation rule itself is clear, the fallback behavior is correct, and the brief-skill schema changes are well-written. The issues are structural: sequencing ambiguity in the extraction step, a missing docs-only guard, and three parallel code paths (`analyze-source`, `create-stack-skill`, `quick-skill`) that were not updated in parallel.

     1→# Skill Brief Schema
     2→
     3→## Purpose
     4→
     5→Defines the output contract for skill-brief.yaml files generated by analyze-source. Each generated brief must conform to this schema to be consumable by brief-skill and create-skill downstream workflows.
     6→
     7→## Required Fields (9)

     9→| Field | Type | Constraint | Description |
    10→|-------|------|------------|-------------|

    12→| version | string | Semantic `X.Y.Z` | Defaults to `1.0.0` |
    13→| source_repo | string | GitHub URL or local path | Repository or project root (optional when `source_type: "docs-only"`) |
    14→| language | string | Recognized language | Primary programming language |
    15→| scope | object | See Scope Object below | Boundary definition |
    16→| description | string | 1-3 sentences | What the skill covers |
    17→| forge_tier | string | `quick` / `forge` / `deep` | Inherited from forge-tier.yaml |
    18→| created | string | ISO date `YYYY-MM-DD` | Generation date |
    19→| created_by | string | user_name from config | Who generated the brief |
    20→
    21→## Optional Fields
    22→
    23→| Field | Type | Constraint | Description |
    24→|-------|------|------------|-------------|
    25→| source_type | string | `source` or `docs-only` | Default `source`. When `docs-only`: `source_repo` optional, `doc_urls` required |
    26→| doc_urls | array | `{url, label}` objects | Documentation URLs for T3 content. Required when `source_type: "docs-only"` |
    27→
    28→## Scope Object
    29→
    30→```yaml
    31→scope:
    32→  type: full-library | specific-modules | public-api | docs-only
    33→  include:
    34→    - 'src/**/*.ts'          # At least one required
    35→  exclude:
    36→    - 'src/**/*.test.ts'     # Optional
    37→  notes: 'Optional rationale for scope decision'
    38→```
    39→
    40→### Scope Types
    41→
    42→| Type | Use When |
    43→|------|----------|
    44→| full-library | Entire codebase of a unit |
    45→| specific-modules | Selected components or packages |
    46→| public-api | Only exported interfaces |
    47→
    48→## YAML Template
    49→
    50→```yaml
    51→name: '{unit-name}'
    52→version: '1.0.0'
    53→source_type: 'source'
    54→source_repo: '{project_path}'
    55→language: '{detected_language}'
    56→scope:
    57→  type: '{detected_scope_type}'
    58→  include:
    59→    - '{include_pattern}'
    60→  exclude:
    61→    - '{exclude_pattern}'
    62→  notes: '{scope_rationale}'
    63→description: '{1-3 sentence description}'
    64→forge_tier: '{forge_tier}'
    65→created: '{current_date}'
    66→created_by: '{user_name}'
    67→```
    68→
    69→## Validation Rules
    70→
    71→1. **Name uniqueness**: No duplicate names within forge_data_folder
    72→2. **Source accessibility**: source_repo path must exist or be reachable
    73→3. **Language recognized**: Must be a known programming language
    74→4. **Scope type valid**: Must match one of the three defined types
    75→5. **Include patterns**: At least one include glob pattern required
    76→6. **Forge tier match**: Must match value in forge-tier.yaml
    77→7. **Docs-only mode**: When `source_type: "docs-only"`, `doc_urls` required (>= 1), `source_repo` optional
    78→
    79→## Output Location
    80→
    81→Each skill-brief.yaml is written to:
    82→```
    83→{forge_data_folder}/{unit-name}/skill-brief.yaml
    84→```
    85→
```

   116→

   118→
   119→⚠️ **CRITICAL:** Load and follow the **AST Extraction Protocol** from `{extractionPatternsData}`. Use the decision tree based on the number of changed files: prefer MCP `find_code()` for small sets, `find_code_by_rule()` with scoped YAML rules for medium sets, and CLI `--json=stream` with line-by-line streaming for large sets. Never use `ast-grep --json` (without `=stream`) — it loads the entire result set into memory and will fail on large codebases.

title:	qmd embed not called after qmd collection add — vector search unavailable
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	33
--
## Steps
`step-03b-fetch-temporal.md` (Section 4), `step-03c-fetch-docs.md` (Section 5b), `step-07-generate-artifacts.md` (Section 5)

## Problem
After every `qmd collection add` command, QMD outputs:

```
Run 'qmd embed' to update embeddings (N unique hashes need vectors)
```

Without running `qmd embed`, only BM25 keyword `search` works. The `vector_search` and `deep_search` tools (which find semantically related content even when vocabulary differs) are non-functional.

None of the workflow step files instruct running `qmd embed` after collection creation. This means:
- Step-04 enrichment using `vector_search` for migration/deprecation context would return no results
- Future audit-skill/update-skill workflows using `deep_search` would be degraded

## Workaround Used
Ran `qmd embed` manually as a background task after temporal collection creation. Other collections were left without embeddings.

## Suggested Fix
After each `qmd collection add` in the workflow, add:

```bash
qmd embed  # or run in background if non-blocking is preferred
```

Alternatively, if BM25 `search` is considered sufficient for the create-skill workflow:
- Document this explicitly: "BM25 search is sufficient for enrichment. Embedding is deferred to export-skill or audit-skill."
- Add `qmd embed` to the export-skill or setup-forge workflow instead.

## Impact
**Degraded enrichment** — `vector_search` and `deep_search` return empty results for QMD collections without embeddings.
```

   112→
   113→### 5b. Index into QMD (Deep Tier Only)
   114→
   115→**If tier is not Deep:** Skip this section silently.
   116→
   117→**If tier is Deep and at least one URL was fetched successfully:**
   118→
   119→1. Write fetched markdown files to a staging directory: `{system_temp}/skf-docs-{skill-name}/`
   120→2. Index into QMD: `qmd collection add {staging-dir}/ --name {skill-name}-docs --mask "*.md"`
   121→3. Register in forge-tier.yaml `qmd_collections` array:
   122→

   124→- name: "{skill-name}-docs"
   125→  type: "docs"
   126→  source_workflow: "create-skill"
   127→  skill_name: "{skill-name}"
   128→  created_at: "{current ISO date}"
   129→```
   130→
   131→4. Clean up staging directory after indexing.
   132→
   133→**If QMD indexing fails:** Warn: "QMD indexing of fetched docs failed. T3 items are still in the extraction inventory — enrichment will proceed without QMD-indexed docs." Continue.

   100→### Example 1: Producer Registration (create-skill)
   101→
   102→**Context:** After writing all skill artifacts to disk, create-skill registers them with QMD.
   103→
   104→**Implementation:** Deep tier only. Atomic replace if collection exists:
   105→
   106→```
   107→qmd collection remove {name}-extraction   (if exists)
   108→qmd collection add skills/{name} --name {name}-extraction --mask "**/*"
   109→```
   110→
   111→Then append/replace the registry entry in forge-tier.yaml. Failures never block the workflow.
   112→
   113→### Example 2: Consumer Discovery (audit-skill)
   114→
   115→**Context:** Audit-skill needs temporal context for drift detection.
   116→
   117→**Implementation:** Read `qmd_collections` from forge-tier.yaml. Find entry where `skill_name` matches AND `type` is `"extraction"`. If found, query via qmd_bridge. If not found, log and continue without T2 enrichment — not an error.
   118→
   119→### Example 3: Janitor Hygiene (setup-forge)
```

title:	Doc fetching step needs subpage discovery for documentation root URLs
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	34
--
## Step
`step-03c-fetch-docs.md` — Section 3 (Fetch Documentation)

## Problem
The workflow instructs: "Fetch the content at `{url}` as clean markdown using the discovered web tool."

When `doc_urls` contains a documentation root URL (e.g., `https://docs.cognee.ai/`), the fetched content is typically just navigation links and a landing page — minimal useful API content. Modern documentation sites (Mintlify, Docusaurus, ReadTheDocs, GitBook) render content on subpages, not the root.

The workflow has no guidance for:
1. Detecting that a root URL returned minimal content
2. Discovering subpages via sitemap, crawl, or map tools
3. Selecting and fetching the most relevant subpages

## Workaround Used
Used `firecrawl_map` to discover 27 subpages, searched for API-relevant terms, then scraped 6 key pages (quickstart, search, cognify, overview, python-api, setup-config) individually.

## Suggested Fix
Add a discovery step after initial fetch:

```
1. Fetch root URL
2. If content < 500 chars of meaningful text (excluding navigation):
   a. Attempt sitemap/map discovery on the root URL
   b. Filter discovered URLs by relevance (search for API, quickstart, reference terms)
   c. Fetch top 5-10 most relevant subpages
3. Aggregate all fetched content for T3 extraction
```

This could also be a brief-level improvement: allow `doc_urls` to specify individual subpages instead of just the root, giving users control over which pages to fetch.

## Impact
**Low T3 yield** — without subpage discovery, doc fetching returns minimal useful content from modern documentation sites, reducing the value of T3 enrichment.
```

     5→---
     6→
     7→# Step 3c: Fetch Remote Documentation
     8→
     9→## STEP GOAL:
    10→
    11→Fetch remote documentation from brief-specified URLs using whatever web fetching capability is available in the agent's environment, extract API information, and add T3-confidence content to the extraction inventory. Tool-agnostic — the agent uses Firecrawl, WebFetch, web-reader, curl, or any available web tool.
    12→
    13→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    14→
    15→### Universal Rules:
    16→
    17→- CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    18→- ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file
    19→- TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread
    20→- YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
    21→
    22→### Role Reinforcement:
    23→
    24→- You are a skill compilation engine fetching external documentation for T3 enrichment
    25→- Doc fetching is best-effort — individual URL failures never block the workflow
    26→- Graceful degradation is paramount — if no web fetching tools are available, skip with a warning and proceed
    27→
    28→### Step-Specific Rules:
    29→
    30→- No tier gate — this step runs at any tier when `doc_urls` are present in the brief
    31→- Tool-agnostic — use whatever web fetching capability is available (Firecrawl, WebFetch, web-reader, curl, browser tools, etc.)
    32→- FORBIDDEN to halt the workflow if web fetching is unavailable or fails
    33→- FORBIDDEN to override existing T1, T1-low, or T2 extraction data with T3 content
    34→- FORBIDDEN to begin compilation — that is step-05
    35→
    36→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    37→
    38→- Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    39→- Every extracted item receives a T3 confidence citation: `[EXT:{url}]`
    40→- Merge doc-fetch results into the extraction inventory per the conflict rule in section 5
    41→- Do NOT hallucinate content — if information is not in the fetched text, exclude it
    42→
    43→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    44→
    45→- Available: brief_data (including `doc_urls`, `source_type`), tier, extraction_inventory from step-03
    46→- Focus: Fetching external documentation and producing T3-confidence extraction items
    47→- Limits: Do NOT modify existing T1/T1-low/T2 items, begin enrichment, or compile content
    48→- Dependencies: Step-03 extraction must be complete (even if inventory is empty for docs-only briefs)
    49→
    50→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    51→
    52→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    53→
    54→### 1. Check Eligibility
    55→
    56→Evaluate the following conditions. **If the condition fails, skip silently to section 7 (auto-proceed) with no output:**
    57→
    58→1. **`doc_urls` is present in the brief data:** Check that `doc_urls` contains at least one URL entry from step-01 context. If `doc_urls` is absent or empty, skip silently.
    59→
    60→No tier gate — if `doc_urls` are present, this step runs at Quick, Forge, and Deep tiers alike.
    61→
    62→### 2. Security Notice
    63→
    64→Display an informational notice (not a gate — the user already approved these URLs in the brief):
    65→
    66→"**Documentation fetch:** The following external URLs will be fetched:
    67→{for each URL: `- {label}: {url}`}
    68→
    69→Content fetched from external URLs is classified as **T3** (external, untrusted) and cited as `[EXT:{url}]`."
    70→
    71→### 3. Fetch Documentation
    72→
    73→**Discover available web fetching capability.** Try tools in any order — use whatever is accessible in the current environment (e.g., Firecrawl scrape, WebFetch, web-reader, MCP fetch, curl, browser tools). If no web fetching capability can be found:
    74→
    75→- Log warning: "No web fetching capability available in this environment. Skipping documentation fetch."
    76→- Skip to section 7 (auto-proceed).
    77→
    78→**For each URL in `doc_urls`:**
    79→
    80→- Fetch the content at `{url}` as clean markdown using the discovered web tool.
    81→- **If fetch succeeds:** Store the markdown content with the URL as provenance source.
    82→- **If fetch fails:** Log warning: "Failed to fetch {url}: {reason}. Skipping." Continue with remaining URLs.
    83→
    84→**If ALL URLs fail:** Log warning: "No documentation could be fetched. Proceeding without T3 content." Skip to section 7 (auto-proceed).
    85→
    86→### 4. Extract API Information from Fetched Content
    87→
    88→Parse the successfully fetched markdown for:
    89→
    90→- **Function/method signatures** and their parameters
    91→- **Return types** and data structures
    92→- **Configuration options** and their defaults
    93→- **Usage examples** and code snippets
    94→
    95→**Citation rule:** Every extracted item gets a T3 confidence citation: `[EXT:{url}]` where `{url}` is the source URL the item was extracted from.
    96→
    97→**No hallucination:** If information cannot be found in the fetched content, exclude it. Do not infer or fabricate API details.
    98→
    99→### 5. Build Doc-Fetch Inventory
   100→
   101→**Mode determines merge behavior:**
   102→
   103→- **`source_type: "docs-only"`** — The doc-fetch inventory IS the extraction inventory. It replaces the empty inventory from step-03, since there was no source code to extract from.
   104→- **`source_type: "source"` (supplemental mode)** — Merge T3 items into the existing extraction inventory from step-03.
   105→
   106→**Conflict rule:** T3 items NEVER override existing T1, T1-low, or T2 items for the same export. When an export already has a higher-confidence entry, the T3 item is discarded. T3 has the lowest priority.
   107→
   108→**Aggregate totals for reporting:**
   109→- URLs fetched successfully vs. total
   110→- URLs that failed
   111→- T3 items extracted
   112→
   113→### 5b. Index into QMD (Deep Tier Only)
   114→
   115→**If tier is not Deep:** Skip this section silently.
   116→
   117→**If tier is Deep and at least one URL was fetched successfully:**
   118→
   119→1. Write fetched markdown files to a staging directory: `{system_temp}/skf-docs-{skill-name}/`
   120→2. Index into QMD: `qmd collection add {staging-dir}/ --name {skill-name}-docs --mask "*.md"`
   121→3. Generate embeddings: `qmd embed` (required for `vector_search` and `deep_search`)
   122→4. Register in forge-tier.yaml `qmd_collections` array:
   123→
   124→```yaml
   125→- name: "{skill-name}-docs"
   126→  type: "docs"
   127→  source_workflow: "create-skill"
   128→  skill_name: "{skill-name}"
   129→  created_at: "{current ISO date}"
   130→```
   131→
   132→5. Clean up staging directory after indexing.
   133→
   134→**If QMD indexing fails:** Warn: "QMD indexing of fetched docs failed. T3 items are still in the extraction inventory — enrichment will proceed without QMD-indexed docs." Continue.
   135→
   136→### 6. Report
   137→
   138→Display:
   139→
   140→"**Documentation fetch complete.**
   141→**URLs processed:** {fetched}/{total}
   142→**T3 items extracted:** {count}
   143→**Confidence:** All doc-fetched items are T3 — `[EXT:{url}]` citations applied.
   144→{If docs-only mode: '**Mode:** Docs-only — all skill content is T3. source_authority: community'}
   145→
   146→Proceeding to enrichment..."
   147→
   148→### 7. Menu Handling Logic
   149→
   150→**Auto-proceed step — no user interaction.**
   151→
   152→After documentation fetch is complete (or skipped for any reason), immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
   153→
   154→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   155→
   156→- This is an auto-proceed step with no user choices
   157→- No `doc_urls` in brief: skip directly to next step with no output
   158→- No web fetching available: skip with warning then auto-proceed
   159→- All URLs failed: skip with warning then auto-proceed
   160→- Successful fetch: display report then auto-proceed
   161→- All failures degrade gracefully — skip and auto-proceed
   162→
   163→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   164→
   165→ONLY WHEN documentation is fetched and T3 items are merged into the extraction inventory (or the step is skipped due to no `doc_urls`, no web tools, or fetch failures) will you proceed to load `{nextStepFile}` for enrichment.
   166→
   167→---
   168→
   169→## SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   170→
   171→### SUCCESS:
   172→
   173→- No `doc_urls` in brief: skipped silently, auto-proceeded
   174→- `doc_urls` present: each URL fetched using whatever web tool is available
   175→- Individual fetch failures handled gracefully (skip and continue)
   176→- All extracted content cited as T3 with `[EXT:{url}]` provenance
   177→- Existing T1/T1-low/T2 items never overridden by T3 data
   178→- Docs-only mode: doc-fetch inventory correctly replaces empty extraction inventory
   179→- Supplemental mode: T3 items merged into existing inventory respecting conflict rule
   180→- Auto-proceeded to step-04
   181→
   182→### SYSTEM FAILURE:
   183→
   184→- Halting the workflow because web fetching is unavailable or a URL fails
   185→- Including fetched content without `[EXT:{url}]` citations
   186→- Overriding existing higher-confidence extractions (T1, T1-low, T2) with T3 data
   187→- Hardcoding a specific fetching tool instead of being tool-agnostic
   188→- Hallucinating API details not found in the fetched content
   189→- Beginning compilation in this step (that is step-05)
   190→
   191→**Master Rule:** Documentation fetching is best-effort T3 enrichment. Fetch what you can, cite everything as `[EXT:{url}]`, never override higher-confidence data, and move on. Failures degrade gracefully — they never block the skill compilation pipeline.
   192→
```

  "old_string": "**For each URL in `doc_urls`:**\n\n- Fetch the content at `{url}` as clean markdown using the discovered web tool.\n- **If fetch succeeds:** Store the markdown content with the URL as provenance source.\n- **If fetch fails:** Log warning: \"Failed to fetch {url}: {reason}. Skipping.\" Continue with remaining URLs.\n\n**If ALL URLs fail:** Log warning: \"No documentation could be fetched. Proceeding without T3 content.\" Skip to section 7 (auto-proceed).",
  "new_string": "**For each URL in `doc_urls`:**\n\n- Fetch the content at `{url}` as clean markdown using the discovered web tool.\n- **If fetch succeeds:** Store the markdown content with the URL as provenance source.\n- **If fetch fails:** Log warning: \"Failed to fetch {url}: {reason}. Skipping.\" Continue with remaining URLs.\n\n**Subpage discovery (root URL detection):**\n\nAfter fetching a URL, check if the returned content is a documentation root with minimal useful API content (e.g., mostly navigation links, fewer than 500 characters of meaningful text excluding navigation/menu items). This is common with modern documentation sites (Mintlify, Docusaurus, ReadTheDocs, GitBook) that render API content on subpages.\n\n**If a root URL with minimal content is detected:**\n\n1. **Attempt sitemap/map discovery:** Use whatever discovery tool is available:\n   - Firecrawl: `firecrawl_map({url})` to discover all subpages\n   - Manual: try fetching `{url}/sitemap.xml` and parsing URLs from it\n   - Crawl: if a crawl tool is available, use it with depth=1 on the root URL\n   - If no discovery tool is available, keep the root page content as-is and continue\n\n2. **Filter discovered URLs by relevance:** From the discovered subpage list, select the most relevant pages by searching for API-related terms in the URL path or title (e.g., `api`, `reference`, `quickstart`, `setup`, `config`, `getting-started`, `guide`, `sdk`, `methods`, `functions`). Exclude pages that are clearly non-API content (e.g., `blog`, `changelog`, `pricing`, `about`, `careers`).\n\n3. **Fetch top subpages:** Fetch up to **10** of the most relevant subpages. For each:\n   - Use the same web fetching tool as the root URL\n   - Store with the subpage URL as provenance: `[EXT:{subpage-url}]`\n   - If a subpage fetch fails, skip it and continue\n\n4. **Rate limiting:** If rate limiting (HTTP 429) is encountered during subpage fetching, stop discovery for this root URL. Keep results collected so far. Log: \"Subpage discovery stopped due to rate limiting.\"\n\n**If ALL URLs fail (including any subpage fetches):** Log warning: \"No documentation could be fetched. Proceeding without T3 content.\" Skip to section 7 (auto-proceed)."
}

title:	Two-tier content design penalized by tessl conciseness scorer
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	35
--
## Step
`step-05-compile.md` — Assembly Rules (Tier 1 + Tier 2)

## Problem
The workflow's two-tier design intentionally places:
- **Tier 1:** Key API Summary (table with name, purpose, key params)
- **Tier 2:** Full API Reference (complete signatures, parameter tables, examples)

tessl's `conciseness` scorer (in step-06) penalizes this as "redundant information — the Full API Reference section largely duplicates the Key API Summary table" (scored 2/3), dropping the content score to 65%.

This is a fundamental tension: the two-tier design exists so SKILL.md is useful standalone (Tier 1) while providing full detail when needed (Tier 2). But the validator sees overlap as redundancy.

## Observed Scores
- Description: 100%
- Content: 65% (conciseness 2/3, progressive_disclosure 2/3)
- Average: 83%

tessl suggestion: "Remove or consolidate the duplicate information between Key API Summary table and Full API Reference section"

## Suggested Fix
Options (not mutually exclusive):

1. **Differentiation guidance in step-05:** Add explicit instruction: "Tier 2 Full API Reference must include content NOT in Tier 1: full parameter tables with types/defaults, return value details, T2 temporal annotations, and usage examples. Avoid repeating Tier 1's name/purpose/key-params format."

2. **Document expected tessl scores:** Add a note in step-06: "tessl conciseness scores of 2/3 are expected for the two-tier design. Content scores >= 60% are acceptable when the two-tier structure is correctly applied."

3. **Force split-body earlier:** If Tier 2 were always extracted to `references/`, tessl would only see Tier 1 content and wouldn't flag redundancy. Consider lowering the `max-body-lines` threshold or making split-body mandatory when both tiers exist.

## Impact
**Cosmetic** — the skill works correctly, but the tessl content score appears lower than warranted, which could mislead users into unnecessary content changes.
```

    28→### Step-Specific Rules:
    29→
    30→- 🎯 Focus ONLY on validating compiled content against spec
    31→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to add new content — only fix spec compliance issues
    32→- 💾 Validation and auto-fix modify files in the staging directory (`_bmad-output/{name}/`)
    33→- 💬 If auto-fix fails, report issues clearly but proceed (warn, don't halt)
    34→- ⚙️ If skill-check unavailable: skip validation, add warning to evidence report
    35→- ⚠️ Ignore non-zero exit codes from `skill-check` if the JSON output shows 0 errors — parse JSON output, not exit codes
    36→
    37→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    38→
    39→- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    40→- 💾 Validation results are added to evidence-report content in context
    41→- 📖 Auto-fix pattern: validate → fix → re-validate (once)
    42→- 🚫 Maximum one auto-fix attempt per validation failure
    43→
    44→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    45→
    46→- Available: All compiled content from step-05 (SKILL.md, metadata.json, etc.)
    47→- Focus: Spec compliance validation and auto-fix
    48→- Limits: Do NOT add new content or modify extraction data
    49→- Dependencies: Compiled content must exist from step-05
    50→
    51→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    52→
    53→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    54→
    55→### 1. Check Tool Availability
    56→
    57→Run: `npx skill-check -h`
    58→
    59→- If succeeds: Continue to automated validation (section 2)
    60→- If fails: Perform manual fallback (section 3); add note to evidence-report: "Spec validation performed manually — skill-check tool unavailable"
    61→
    62→**Important:** Do not assume availability — empirical check required.
    63→
    64→### 2. Validate & Auto-Fix (skill-check check --fix)
    65→
    66→Run the external skill-check tool against the compiled skill staging directory:
    67→
    68→```bash
    69→npx skill-check check <staging-skill-dir> --fix --format json --no-security-scan
    70→```
    71→
    72→This performs frontmatter validation, description quality checks, body limit enforcement, local link resolution, file formatting, auto-fix of deterministic issues, and quality scoring (0-100) across five weighted categories.
    73→
    74→**Parse the JSON output** for: `qualityScore` (0-100), `diagnostics[]` (remaining issues), `fixed[]` (auto-corrected issues).
    75→
    76→**Note:** `skill-check` may return non-zero exit code even when `errorCount` is 0. Always rely on parsed JSON, not the shell exit code.
    77→
    78→- **Score ≥ 70:** Record "Schema: PASS (score: {score}/100)" in evidence-report
    79→- **Score < 70:** Log remaining diagnostics as warnings, record "Schema: WARN — score {score}/100, {count} remaining issues", proceed
    80→- **Unfixable errors:** Record specific rule IDs and suggestions, proceed with warnings
    81→
    82→### 3. Validate Frontmatter (Fallback)
    83→
    84→**If skill-check was available:** Skip — already validated in step 2.
    85→
    86→**If skill-check NOT available (fallback):** Perform manual frontmatter compliance check:
    87→
    88→- [ ] Frontmatter present — file starts with `---` and has closing `---`
    89→- [ ] `name` field — present, non-empty, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens only, 1-64 chars
    90→- [ ] `name` matches skill output directory name
    91→- [ ] `description` field — present, non-empty, 1-1024 characters
    92→- [ ] No unknown fields — only `name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools` permitted
    93→- [ ] `version` and `author` are NOT in frontmatter (they belong in metadata.json)
    94→
    95→If fails: auto-fix (deterministic), re-validate once, record result. If passes: record "Frontmatter: PASS".
    96→
    97→### 4. Split Oversized Body (if needed)
    98→
    99→**If step 2 reported `body.max_lines` failure:**
   100→
   101→```bash
   102→npx skill-check split-body <staging-skill-dir> --write
   103→```
   104→
   105→Then re-validate: `npx skill-check check <staging-skill-dir> --format json --no-security-scan`
   106→
   107→**If skill-check unavailable or no body size issue:** Skip.
   108→
   109→### 5. Security Scan
   110→
   111→**If skill-check available:**
   112→
   113→```bash
   114→npx skill-check check <staging-skill-dir> --format json
   115→```
   116→
   117→(Security scan enabled by default when `--no-security-scan` omitted.)
   118→
   119→Record any security warnings in evidence-report. Security findings are advisory — they do not block artifact generation.
   120→
   121→**If unavailable:** Skip with note: "Security scan skipped — skill-check tool unavailable"
   122→
   123→### 6. Content Quality Review (tessl)
   124→
   125→**If tessl available**, run: `npx -y tessl skill review <staging-skill-dir>`
   126→
   127→Parse output for: `description_score`, `content_score`, `average_score`, `validation_result`, `judge_suggestions[]`.
   128→
   129→- **Content score < 70%:** Record warning: "Content quality warning: tessl scored content at {score}%. Consider inlining Quick Start and common workflows directly in SKILL.md."
   130→- **Unavailable:** Skip with note: "Content quality review skipped — tessl tool unavailable"
   131→
   132→tessl installs automatically via `npx`. A missing tool is not an error — graceful skip.
   133→
   134→### 7. Validate metadata.json
   135→
   136→Cross-check metadata.json against extraction inventory:
   137→- `stats.exports_documented` / `stats.exports_total` / `stats.coverage` are accurate
   138→- `confidence_t1`, `confidence_t2`, `confidence_t3` match actual counts
   139→- `spec_version` is "1.3"
   140→
   141→Auto-fix any discrepancies (these are computed values).
   142→
   143→### 8. Update Evidence Report
   144→
   145→Add validation results to evidence-report content in context:
   146→
   147→```markdown
   148→## Validation Results
   149→- Schema: {pass/fail} (quality score: {score}/100)
   150→- Frontmatter: {pass/fail}
   151→- Body: {pass/fail} {split-body applied if applicable}
   152→- Security: {pass/warn/skipped}
   153→- Content Quality (tessl): {pass/warn/skipped} (score: {score}%)
   154→- Metadata: {pass/fail}
   155→
   156→## Quality Score Breakdown
   157→- Frontmatter (30%): {score} | Description (30%): {score} | Body (20%): {score} | Links (10%): {score} | File (10%): {score}
   158→
   159→## Auto-Fixed Issues
   160→- {list of issues automatically corrected by --fix}
   161→
   162→## Remaining Warnings / Security Findings / Content Quality (tessl)
   163→- {warnings, security results, tessl scores and suggestions — or "skipped"}
   164→```
   165→
   166→### 9. Menu Handling Logic
   167→
   168→**Auto-proceed step — no user interaction.**
   169→
   170→After validation completes (or is skipped if tools unavailable), immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
   171→
   172→- Tool unavailability is a skip, not a halt
   173→- Validation failures are warnings — proceed to artifact generation
   174→
   175→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   176→
   177→ONLY WHEN validation is complete (or skipped) and evidence-report content is updated will you proceed to load `{nextStepFile}` for artifact generation.
   178→
   179→---
   180→
   181→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   182→
   183→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   184→
   185→- `npx skill-check check --fix --format json` executed (or skipped with warning if unavailable)
   186→- Quality score captured and recorded; auto-fix applied for deterministic issues
   187→- Split-body applied if `body.max_lines` failed; security scan executed (or skipped with warning)
   188→- `npx -y tessl skill review` executed (or skipped); content quality warning raised if score < 70%
   189→- Metadata cross-check performed; evidence report updated with structured results
   190→- Auto-proceeded to step-07
   191→
   192→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   193→
   194→- Halting on validation failure or skill-check unavailability (should warn and proceed)
   195→- Adding new content during validation (only structural fixes allowed)
   196→- Not recording quality score; skipping security scan without recording the skip
   197→- Attempting more than one auto-fix cycle per failure
   198→
   199→**Master Rule:** Validation informs, it does not block. Record results, fix what's deterministic, scan for security issues, warn about the rest, and proceed.
   200→
```

Specifically:\n\n- **Full parameter tables** with types, defaults, and required/optional flags (Tier 1 only lists key params)\n- **Return value details** including structure, types, and error conditions\n- **T2 temporal annotations** — migration notes, deprecation details, breaking change context (Deep tier)\n- **Usage examples** from source tests or documentation (Tier 1 has signature-only references)\n- **Edge cases and constraints** — parameter validation rules, size limits, behavioral notes\n\nDo NOT repeat Tier 1's name/purpose/key-params table format in Tier 2. title:	AST Extraction Protocol file count ambiguity — total repo vs filtered scope
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	36
--
## Step
`step-03-extract.md` + `data/extraction-patterns.md` — AST Extraction Protocol Decision Tree

## Problem
The decision tree states:

```
Files in scope ≤ 100  → MCP find_code()
Files in scope 101–500  → MCP find_code_by_rule()
Files in scope > 500  → CLI streaming fallback
```

But "files in scope" is ambiguous. During the cognee build:
- **Total repo files** (from `gh api .../git/trees`): 2,202
- **Files after scope filtering** (Python files matching include/exclude patterns): 958

These lead to different strategies. The total repo count would always trigger CLI streaming for large repos, even when the actual files in scope are moderate.

## Workaround Used
Used the filtered count (958 Python files in scope) as the decision input, which selected CLI streaming. This seemed correct — AST extraction only processes files matching the brief's scope patterns.

## Suggested Fix
Clarify in `extraction-patterns.md`:

```
"Files in scope" = files remaining after applying include_patterns and 
exclude_patterns from the brief, filtered by the target language extension. 
This is NOT the total repository file count from step-01's tree listing.
```

Additionally, step-03 section 2 (Apply Scope Filters) should explicitly output: "Filtered file count: {N} files in scope" to make the decision tree input unambiguous.

## Impact
**Strategy selection** — using total repo count instead of filtered count could select an unnecessarily conservative strategy (CLI streaming when MCP tools would suffice), or vice versa.
```

> AGENT

Straightforward clarification needed in two places. Let me read the relevant sections.

title:	Add user decision gate after tessl content quality review in step-06
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	37
--
## Step
`step-06-validate.md` — Section 6 (Content Quality Review)

## Problem
The tessl content quality review produces actionable suggestions (e.g., "add validation checkpoints", "split Full API Reference to separate file", "add error handling examples"), but the workflow treats the output as purely advisory — it records the score and auto-proceeds to artifact generation with no user interaction.

This means:
1. **Useful improvement opportunities are silently skipped** — tessl's suggestions could meaningfully improve the skill, but the user never gets to decide
2. **No informed consent for semantic changes** — some suggestions (like "add error handling examples") would require generating content not found in source code, potentially violating the zero-hallucination principle
3. **The score is recorded but never acted upon** — the 65% content score in the evidence report is informational only

## Current Behavior
```
tessl review → record score → auto-proceed to step-07
```

## Expected Behavior
```
tessl review → present suggestions → user decision gate → apply/skip → proceed to step-07
```

## Suggested Fix
Add a **conditional user decision gate** after tessl review (only when suggestions exist):

```markdown
**Content quality review: {score}%**

tessl suggestions:
- {suggestion_1}
- {suggestion_2}
- ...

**Select an option:**
[A] Auto-fix — apply tessl suggestions
    ⚠️ Warning: some fixes may add content not verified from source code.
    Applied changes will be cited as [TESSL:suggestion] (unverified).
[S] Skip — proceed with current content as-is
[R] Review — show proposed changes before applying
```

### Gate Rules
- **Only display when tessl produces suggestions** — if tessl returns no suggestions, auto-proceed silently
- **Auto-fix applies only structural suggestions** (split reference section, consolidate duplicates) without user confirmation
- **Semantic suggestions** (add examples, add error handling, add validation checkpoints) require explicit user approval and must carry a warning that content may not be source-verified
- **If user selects [A]:** Applied content should be marked with `<!-- [TESSL:auto-fix] -->` markers so update-skill can distinguish generated patches from source-verified content
- **Content score threshold:** Consider only triggering the gate when content score < 70% (configurable)

### Zero-Hallucination Consideration
Some tessl suggestions directly conflict with the zero-hallucination principle:
- "Add error handling examples showing how to catch CogneeApiError" → requires inventing code
- "Add validation checkpoints" → requires inventing workflow steps

The gate must clearly warn the user that applying these changes introduces unverified content. The provenance map should mark these entries as `confidence: "TESSL"` (below T3) to signal they are LLM-suggested improvements, not source-derived facts.

## Impact
**Missed improvement opportunity** — the skill ships at 65% content quality when user-approved fixes could bring it to 80%+, with informed consent about the tradeoff.
```

   122→
   123→### 6. Content Quality Review (tessl)
   124→
   125→**If tessl available**, run: `npx -y tessl skill review <staging-skill-dir>`
   126→
   127→Parse output for: `description_score`, `content_score`, `average_score`, `validation_result`, `judge_suggestions[]`.
   128→
   129→- **Content score < 70%:** Record warning: "Content quality warning: tessl scored content at {score}%. Consider inlining Quick Start and common workflows directly in SKILL.md."
   130→- **Unavailable:** Skip with note: "Content quality review skipped — tessl tool unavailable"
   131→
   132→**Expected scoring for two-tier design:** The Skill Forge two-tier design (Tier 1 Key API Summary + Tier 2 Full API Reference) intentionally includes progressive disclosure. tessl's `conciseness` scorer may flag this as redundancy (typically scoring 2/3), which is expected behavior — not a defect. Content scores >= 60% are acceptable when both tiers are correctly applied with proper differentiation (see step-05 Tier 2 assembly rules). Do NOT consolidate Tier 1 and Tier 2 content to improve the score — the two-tier structure is a deliberate design choice for standalone usability.

   168→### 9. Menu Handling Logic
   169→
   170→**Auto-proceed step — no user interaction.**
   171→

   173→

   188→- Quality score captured and recorded; auto-fix applied for deterministic issues
   189→- Split-body applied if `body.max_lines` failed; security scan executed (or skipped with warning)

   197→- Adding new content during validation (only structural fixes allowed)
   198→- Not recording quality score; skipping security scan without recording the skip
   199→- Attempting more than one auto-fix cycle per failure
   200→
   201→**Master Rule:** Validation informs, it does not block. Record results, fix what's deterministic, scan for security issues, warn about the rest, and proceed.

Content Quality Review (tessl)\n\n**If tessl available**, run: `npx -y tessl skill review <staging-skill-dir>`\n\nParse output for: `description_score`, `content_score`, `average_score`, `validation_result`, `judge_suggestions[]`.\n\n- **Content score < 70%:** Record warning: \"Content quality warning: tessl scored content at {score}%. Consider inlining Quick Start and common workflows directly in SKILL.md.\"\n- **Unavailable:** Skip with note: \"Content quality review skipped — tessl tool unavailable\"\n\n**Expected scoring for two-tier design:** The Skill Forge two-tier design (Tier 1 Key API Summary + Tier 2 Full API Reference) intentionally includes progressive disclosure. tessl's `conciseness` scorer may flag this as redundancy (typically scoring 2/3), which is expected behavior — not a defect. Content scores >= 60% are acceptable when both tiers are correctly applied with proper differentiation (see step-05 Tier 2 assembly rules). Do NOT consolidate Tier 1 and Tier 2 content to improve the score — the two-tier structure is a deliberate design choice for standalone usability.\n\ntessl installs automatically via `npx`. A missing tool is not an error — graceful skip.",
  "new_string": "### 6. Content Quality Review (tessl)\n\n**If tessl available**, run: `npx -y tessl skill review <staging-skill-dir>`\n\nParse output for: `description_score`, `content_score`, `average_score`, `validation_result`, `judge_suggestions[]`.\n\n- **Content score < 70%:** Record warning: \"Content quality warning: tessl scored content at {score}%.\"\n- **Unavailable:** Skip with note: \"Content quality review skipped — tessl tool unavailable\"\n\n**Expected scoring for two-tier design:** The Skill Forge two-tier design (Tier 1 Key API Summary + Tier 2 Full API Reference) intentionally includes progressive disclosure. tessl's `conciseness` scorer may flag this as redundancy (typically scoring 2/3), which is expected behavior — not a defect. Content scores >= 60% are acceptable when both tiers are correctly applied with proper differentiation (see step-05 Tier 2 assembly rules). Do NOT consolidate Tier 1 and Tier 2 content to improve the score — the two-tier structure is a deliberate design choice for standalone usability.\n\ntessl installs automatically via `npx`. A missing tool is not an error — graceful skip.\n\n#### 6b. User Decision Gate (conditional)\n\n**If tessl returned no suggestions OR tessl was unavailable:** Skip this gate — auto-proceed.\n\n**If tessl returned suggestions**, present them to the user:\n\n\"**Content quality review: {score}%**\n\ntessl suggestions:\n{numbered list of judge_suggestions}\n\n**Select an option:**\n- **[S] Skip** — proceed with current content as-is (default)\n- **[A] Apply structural fixes** — apply only structural suggestions (split sections, consolidate duplicates). No new content generated.\n- **[R] Review all** — show each suggestion with proposed changes before applying\"\n\n#### Gate Rules:\n\n- **Structural suggestions** (split reference section, consolidate duplicates, reorder sections) can be applied without zero-hallucination risk — they restructure existing content\n- **Semantic suggestions** (add examples, add error handling, add validation checkpoints) introduce content not verified from source code. If the user chooses to apply these:\n  - Warn: \"This adds content not verified from source code.\"\n  - Mark applied content with `<!-- [TESSL:auto-fix] -->` markers\n  - Cite as `[TESSL:suggestion]` in the provenance map with `confidence: \"TESSL\"` (below T3)\n  - Record in evidence report: \"TESSL-suggested content applied: {count} items (unverified)\"\n- **If user selects [S]:** Record \"tessl suggestions: skipped by user\" in evidence report. Proceed to section 7.\n- **If user selects [A]:** Apply structural fixes only, re-run tessl to capture updated score, record results. Proceed to section 7.\n- **If user selects [R]:** Show each suggestion with the proposed change. For each, user confirms or skips. Apply confirmed changes, record results. Proceed to section 7."
}
```

title:	context-snippet.md format should follow Vercel agents.md research findings
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	38
--
## Steps
`data/skill-sections.md` — context-snippet.md Format (ADR-L v2)
`step-05-compile.md` — Section 3 (Build context-snippet.md Content)

## Problem
The current context-snippet.md format is a passive label that describes the skill but doesn't guide agent behavior. Vercel's research ([agents.md outperforms skills in our agent evals](https://vercel.com/blog/agents-md-outperforms-skills-in-our-agent-evals)) found that specific structural patterns dramatically improve agent performance:

1. **Retrieval instruction** — explicit directive to prefer docs over training data
2. **Root pointer** — actionable path to retrievable files
3. **Indexed file map** — section-to-file mapping so agents know where to look
4. **Version pinning** — signals training data may be stale
5. **Inline gotchas** — prevent common mistakes without requiring a file read

### Current format (from `skill-sections.md`):
```
{skill-name} → skills/{skill-name}/
  {compressed description ~15 words from SKILL.md frontmatter}
  exports: {comma-separated top 10 function names}
  refs: {reference-file-names without .md extension}
```

### Problems with current format:
- **No retrieval instruction** — agent has no signal to read SKILL.md instead of relying on training data
- **No root pointer** — `→ skills/cognee/` is descriptive, not actionable ("here's where it is" vs "read this")
- **Exports list is pre-training-friendly** — listing function names the model likely already knows; doesn't highlight unstable/changed API surface
- **refs line is conceptual** — lists reference names that may not map to actual files (e.g., when split-body doesn't trigger)
- **No version pinning** — no signal that training data may be stale for this library version
- **No inline gotchas** — common mistakes (all functions async, breaking changes) require reading the full SKILL.md

## Suggested Fix

Update the context-snippet.md format in `skill-sections.md` to align with Vercel's indexed format:

```markdown
## context-snippet.md Format (Vercel-aligned)

[{skill-name} v{version}]|root: skills/{skill-name}/
|IMPORTANT: {skill-name} v{version} — read SKILL.md before writing {skill-name} code. Do NOT rely on training data.
|quick-start:{SKILL.md#quick-start}
|api:{comma-separated top exports with () for functions}
|key-types:{SKILL.md#key-types} — {inline summary of most important type values}
|config:{SKILL.md#section} — {inline summary}
|gotchas: {2-3 most common mistakes or breaking changes, inline}
```

### Format rules:
- **Line 1:** Skill name + version + root path — version signals training data staleness
- **Line 2 (IMPORTANT):** Retrieval instruction — always present, tells agent to read before acting
- **Lines 3+:** Pipe-delimited index mapping topics to SKILL.md sections (with `#anchor` pointers)
- **Inline content:** Each index line includes a brief inline summary (~10 words) so the agent can decide whether to read the full section
- **gotchas line:** 2-3 most critical pitfalls inline — prevents mistakes without requiring a file read
- **Token budget:** ~80-120 tokens per skill (up from ~50-80, justified by Vercel's finding that indexed format maintains performance at 80% compression)

### Compilation changes needed in `step-05-compile.md`:
- Section 3 should derive gotchas from: T2-future annotations (breaking changes), async requirement, and any version-specific behavior changes
- Section anchors (`#quick-start`, `#key-types`) should be verified against actual SKILL.md headings
- Version should come from source (not brief) per issue #32

### Example — cognee skill:
```
[cognee v0.5.5]|root: skills/cognee/
|IMPORTANT: cognee API changed significantly in v0.5.x — always read SKILL.md before suggesting cognee code. Do NOT rely on training data for cognee patterns.
|quick-start:{SKILL.md#quick-start}
|api: add(), cognify(), search(), config, prune, datasets, run_custom_pipeline(), memify()
|search-types:{SKILL.md#key-types} — 14 modes: GRAPH_COMPLETION(default), RAG_COMPLETION, CHUNKS, SUMMARIES, TEMPORAL, CYPHER...
|config:{SKILL.md#architecture-at-a-glance} — LLM/vector/graph provider setup
|gotchas: all functions async, DLT write disposition changed to 'replace' in v0.5.5
```

## Research Reference
Vercel blog: [agents.md outperforms skills in our agent evals](https://vercel.com/blog/agents-md-outperforms-skills-in-our-agent-evals)

Key findings:
- Passive context (always available) outperforms active retrieval (skill invocation)
- 80% size reduction with indexed format maintained perfect performance
- Explicit retrieval instructions ("prefer docs over training data") dramatically improved results
- Removing agent decision points about whether to consult docs improved consistency

## Impact
**Agent effectiveness** — the context-snippet is injected into CLAUDE.md and is the primary discovery signal for skills. A Vercel-aligned format would shift agents from "I know cognee from training" to "let me read the current docs" — the exact behavior that Vercel's evals showed produces better results.
```

     1→# SKILL.md Section Structure
     2→
     3→## agentskills.io Compliant Format
     4→
     5→### Frontmatter (Required — agentskills.io Compliance)
     6→
     7→```yaml
     8→---
     9→name: {skill-name}
    10→description: >
    11→  {Trigger-optimized description of what the skill does and when to use it.
    12→  Include specific keywords for agent discovery.
    13→  Mention what NOT to use it for if applicable.
    14→  1-1024 characters.}
    15→---
    16→```
    17→
    18→**Frontmatter rules (agentskills.io specification):**
    19→
    20→- `name`: 1-64 characters, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens only, must match parent directory name
    21→- `description`: 1-1024 characters, trigger-optimized for agent matching
    22→- Only 6 fields permitted: `name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools`
    23→- `version` and `author` belong in metadata.json, NOT in frontmatter
    24→
    25→### Two-Tier Section Structure
    26→
    27→SKILL.md uses a two-tier structure to ensure actionable content survives `split-body` extraction.
    28→
    29→#### Tier 1 — Always Inline (survives split-body, target <300 lines)
    30→
    31→| # | Section | Budget | Purpose |
    32→|---|---------|--------|---------|
    33→| 1 | **Overview** | ~10 lines | What the library does, source repo, version, tier, export count |
    34→| 2 | **Quick Start** | ~30 lines | 3-5 core functions with one runnable end-to-end example |
    35→| 3 | **Common Workflows** | ~30 lines | 4-5 typical function call sequences for common tasks |
    36→| 4 | **Key API Summary** | ~20 lines | Table of top 10-15 functions (name, purpose, key params) |
    37→| 4b | **Migration & Deprecation Warnings** | ~10 lines | T2-future warnings inline (Deep tier only, skip if none) |
    38→| 5 | **Key Types** | ~20 lines | Most important enum/type values inline |
    39→| 6 | **Architecture at a Glance** | ~10 lines | Bullet list of subsystem categories |
    40→| 7 | **CLI** | ~10 lines | Basic CLI commands (skip if no CLI) |
    41→| 8 | **Manual Sections** | ~5 lines | `<!-- [MANUAL] -->` markers for update-skill |
    42→
    43→#### Tier 2 — Reference-Eligible (extracted by split-body into references/)
    44→
    45→| # | Section | Purpose |
    46→|---|---------|---------|
    47→| 9 | **Full API Reference** | Complete signatures, parameter tables, return types, examples, citations |
    48→| 10 | **Full Type Definitions** | All types, interfaces, enums with full field details |
    49→| 11 | **Full Integration Patterns** | Co-import patterns, adapter details, pipeline internals (Forge/Deep only) |
    50→

    51→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    52→
    53→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
    54→

    70→

```
     1→# Context Snippet Format (ADR-L v2)
     2→
     3→## Format Rules
     4→
     5→- Consistent multi-line format per skill — no adaptive switching
     6→- ~50-80 tokens per skill target (description + exports + optional refs)
     7→- T1-now content only (AST-current, no annotations)
     8→- Description line is the WHEN-TO-USE signal — compressed from SKILL.md frontmatter
     9→- Refs line is the WHERE-TO-LOOK signal — only when references/ exists
    10→
    11→## Single Skill Snippet Template
    12→
    13→```markdown
    14→{skill-name} → skills/{skill-name}/
    15→  {compressed-description — ~15 words from SKILL.md frontmatter description}
    16→  exports: {export-1}, {export-2}, {export-3}, {export-4}, {export-5}
    17→  refs: {ref-1}, {ref-2}, {ref-3}
    18→```
    19→
    20→- **Line 1:** Name + path pointer (always present)
    21→- **Line 2:** Compressed description from SKILL.md frontmatter `description:` field (~15 words, trigger-optimized)
    22→- **Line 3:** Top exports from metadata.json `exports` array (up to 10 for Deep tier, 5 otherwise)
    23→- **Line 4:** Reference file names without extensions (only when `references/` directory exists and contains files)
    24→
    25→## Stack Skill Snippet Template
    26→
    27→```markdown
    28→{project}-stack → skills/{project}-stack/
    29→  {compressed-description}
    30→  stack: {dep-1}@{v1}, {dep-2}@{v2}, {dep-3}@{v3}
    31→  integrations: {pattern-1}, {pattern-2}, {pattern-3}
    32→```
    33→
    34→## Rules
    35→
    36→- **description**: Compress SKILL.md frontmatter `description:` to ~15 words. Focus on WHAT it does and WHEN to use it.
    37→- **exports**: Top exports from metadata.json `exports` array (by order as listed)
    38→- **refs**: File names from `references/` directory, without `.md` extension, sorted. Omit line if no references/ exists.
    39→- **stack**: Component versions from metadata.json `components` for stack skills
    40→- **integrations**: Co-import patterns from metadata.json `integrations` for stack skills
    41→- If fewer exports than the limit, list all available
    42→- If no exports data available, omit the exports line
    43→- Skill path is relative to project root
    44→

  "new_string": "## context-snippet.md Format (Vercel-aligned indexed format)\n\nIndexed pipe-delimited format for CLAUDE.md managed section (~80-120 tokens per skill). Aligned with Vercel's research finding that retrieval instructions + indexed file maps + inline gotchas dramatically improve agent performance.\n\n```markdown\n[{skill-name} v{version}]|root: skills/{skill-name}/\n|IMPORTANT: {skill-name} v{version} — read SKILL.md before writing {skill-name} code. Do NOT rely on training data.\n|quick-start:{SKILL.md#quick-start}\n|api: {top exports with () for functions, comma-separated}\n|key-types:{SKILL.md#key-types} — {inline summary of most important type values}\n|gotchas: {2-3 most critical pitfalls or breaking changes, inline}\n```\n\n### Format rules\n\n- **Line 1:** Skill name + version + root path — version signals training data staleness\n- **Line 2 (IMPORTANT):** Retrieval instruction — always present, tells agent to read SKILL.md before acting\n- **Lines 3+:** Pipe-delimited index mapping topics to SKILL.md sections (with `#anchor` pointers)\n- **Inline content:** Each index line includes a brief inline summary (~10 words) so the agent can decide whether to read the full section\n- **gotchas line:** 2-3 most critical pitfalls inline — prevents mistakes without requiring a file read\n- **Token budget:** ~80-120 tokens per skill (justified by Vercel's finding that indexed format maintains performance at 80% compression)\n- **T1-now content only** — no T2 annotations in the snippet\n- **Section anchors** (`#quick-start`, `#key-types`) must match actual SKILL.md heading slugs\n- **Version** comes from source detection (per issue #32), not brief default"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/export-skill/data/snippet-format.md",
  "content": "# Context Snippet Format (Vercel-Aligned Indexed Format)\n\n## Format Rules\n\n- Indexed pipe-delimited format per skill — retrieval instruction + file map + inline gotchas\n- ~80-120 tokens per skill target (up from ~50-80, justified by Vercel research)\n- T1-now content only (AST-current, no annotations)\n- Line 2 (IMPORTANT) is the RETRIEVAL INSTRUCTION — always present, tells agent to read SKILL.md\n- Section anchors (`#quick-start`, `#key-types`) must match actual SKILL.md heading slugs\n- gotchas line prevents common mistakes without requiring a file read\n- Version comes from source detection, not brief default\n\n## Single Skill Snippet Template\n\n```markdown\n[{skill-name} v{version}]|root: skills/{skill-name}/\n|IMPORTANT: {skill-name} v{version} — read SKILL.md before writing {skill-name} code. Do NOT rely on training data.\n|quick-start:{SKILL.md#quick-start}\n|api: {top exports with () for functions, comma-separated}\n|key-types:{SKILL.md#key-types} — {inline summary of most important type values}\n|gotchas: {2-3 most critical pitfalls or breaking changes, inline}\n```\n\n- **Line 1:** Skill name + version + root path — version signals training data staleness\n- **Line 2 (IMPORTANT):** Retrieval instruction — always present\n- **Line 3 (quick-start):** Anchor pointer to Quick Start section\n- **Line 4 (api):** Top exports from metadata.json `exports` array (up to 10, with `()` for functions)\n- **Line 5 (key-types):** Anchor pointer + inline summary of most important type/enum values\n- **Line 6 (gotchas):** 2-3 most critical pitfalls — derived from T2-future annotations, async requirements, breaking changes\n\n## Stack Skill Snippet Template\n\n```markdown\n[{project}-stack v{version}]|root: skills/{project}-stack/\n|IMPORTANT: {project}-stack — read SKILL.md before writing integration code. Do NOT rely on training data.\n|stack: {dep-1}@{v1}, {dep-2}@{v2}, {dep-3}@{v3}\n|integrations: {pattern-1}, {pattern-2}\n|gotchas: {2-3 most critical integration pitfalls, inline}\n```\n\n## Rules\n\n- **api line**: Top exports from metadata.json `exports` array. Append `()` to function names. Comma-separated.\n- **key-types line**: Anchor to `#key-types` section + inline summary (~10 words) of the most important type values\n- **gotchas line**: Derived from: T2-future annotations (breaking changes), async requirements, version-specific behavior. If no gotchas available, omit the line.\n- **stack line**: Component versions from metadata.json `components` for stack skills\n- **integrations line**: Co-import patterns from metadata.json `integrations` for stack skills\n- If fewer exports than the limit, list all available\n- If no exports data available, omit the api line\n- Section anchors must be verified against actual SKILL.md headings during generation\n- Skill path is relative to project root\n"
}

title:	Document Snyk Agent Scan setup for skill-check security scanning
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	39
--
## Steps affected
- `step-01-detect-and-tier.md` (setup-forge workflow) — tool detection
- `step-06-validate.md` (create-skill workflow) — security scan execution
- SKF README / onboarding documentation

## Problem
When `skill-check check` runs without `--no-security-scan`, it invokes **Snyk Agent Scan** (formerly `mcp-scan`) which requires a `SNYK_TOKEN` environment variable. Without it, the scan fails with:

```
Snyk Agent Scan v0.4.8
To use Agent Scan, set the SNYK_TOKEN environment variable.
To get a token, go to https://app.snyk.io/account (API Token -> KEY -> click to show).

WARNING: The 'mcp-scan' package has been renamed to 'snyk-agent-scan'.
Please update your installation and use 'uvx snyk-agent-scan@latest' instead.
```

This dependency chain is undocumented in the SKF module:
- `skill-check check` → calls `snyk-agent-scan` (via `--security-scan-runner auto|local|uvx|pipx`) → requires `SNYK_TOKEN`
- The token is a free Snyk account API key from https://app.snyk.io/account

### What's missing:

**1. Setup-forge doesn't detect security scan readiness**
The setup-forge workflow (`step-01-detect-and-tier.md`) checks for `ast-grep`, `gh`, and `qmd` but not:
- Whether `SNYK_TOKEN` is set
- Whether `snyk-agent-scan` / `mcp-scan` is installed
- Whether Python is available (required for the scanner)

**2. Step-06 doesn't explain how to enable it**
The validation step says "if unavailable, skip with note" but provides no guidance on HOW to make it available. A user who sees "Security scan skipped" in the evidence report has no actionable path to enable it.

**3. No onboarding documentation**
The SKF README and setup instructions don't mention Snyk or security scanning at all.

## Suggested Fix

### A. Add to setup-forge tool detection (`step-01-detect-and-tier.md`)

Add an optional security scan detection section:

```yaml
# In forge-tier.yaml
tools:
  ast_grep: true
  gh_cli: true
  qmd: true
  security_scan: false  # NEW — true when SNYK_TOKEN is set
```

Detection logic:
```bash
# Check if SNYK_TOKEN is configured
if [ -n "$SNYK_TOKEN" ]; then
  echo "security_scan: true"
else
  echo "security_scan: false"
fi
```

This is informational only — security scan availability should NOT affect the tier level (it's orthogonal to Quick/Forge/Deep).

### B. Add guidance to step-06-validate.md

After the security scan section, add:

```markdown
**If security scan is skipped due to missing token:**

Display: "Security scan requires a Snyk token. To enable:
1. Create a free account at https://app.snyk.io
2. Get your API token from Account Settings → API Token
3. Set `SNYK_TOKEN=your-token` in your environment or `.env`
4. Re-run [CS] Create Skill to include security scanning

Security scanning checks skill files for prompt injection risks, 
sensitive data exposure, and unsafe tool permissions."
```

### C. Add to SKF README / onboarding docs

Add a section under "Optional Tools":

```markdown
### Security Scanning (Optional)
skill-check includes security scanning via [Snyk Agent Scan](https://github.com/snyk/snyk-agent-scan).
To enable:
1. Create a free Snyk account at https://app.snyk.io
2. Copy your API token from Account Settings
3. Add to your environment: `export SNYK_TOKEN=your-token`

Security scanning runs automatically during `skill-check check`. 
Use `--no-security-scan` to skip, or `--security-scan-runner pipx|uvx|local` 
to control the execution method.
```

### D. Reference skill-check documentation

Link to the upstream tool for runner options:
- `--security-scan-runner auto|local|uvx|pipx`
- `--security-scan-verbose` for full output
- `--no-installs` to prevent auto-installation of scanner dependencies
- Incompatibility: security scan is read-only (cannot combine with `--fix`)

Source: https://github.com/thedaviddias/skill-check

## Impact
**Discoverability** — users see "Security scan skipped" with no path to enable it. The Snyk dependency is invisible, making security scanning a hidden feature that most users will never activate.
```

> TOOL
