    21→- 🎯 Execute all operations autonomously — no user interaction
    22→
    23→### Role Reinforcement:
    24→
    25→- ✅ You are a system executor performing environment resolution
    26→- ✅ Tool detection must be empirical — run actual commands, never assume
    27→- ✅ Record exact results (version strings, error messages)
    28→
    29→### Step-Specific Rules:
    30→
    31→- 🎯 Focus only on tool detection and tier calculation
    32→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to write any files — that is step-02's job
    33→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to skip any tool check — all 3 must be verified
    34→- 💬 Tool command failures are NOT errors — they indicate unavailability
    35→
    36→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    37→
    38→- 🎯 Follow the MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    39→- 💾 Store all results in memory for step-02
    40→- 📖 Load tier-rules.md for calculation reference
    41→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to proceed without checking all 3 tools
    42→
    43→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    44→
    45→- This is the first step — no prior context exists
    46→- Available: forge-tier.yaml and preferences.yaml may exist from prior runs
    47→- Focus: tool verification and tier calculation only
    48→- Dependencies: none — this step bootstraps everything
    49→
    50→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    51→
    52→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    53→
    54→### 1. Load Tier Rules
    55→
    56→Load and read {tierRulesData} for the tool detection commands and tier calculation logic.
    57→
    58→### 2. Check for Existing Configuration (Re-run Detection)
    59→
    60→**Read existing forge-tier.yaml** at `{project-root}/_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/forge-tier.yaml`:
    61→- If exists: store the current `tier` value as `{previous_tier}` and `tier_detected_at` as `{previous_detection_date}`
    62→- If not found: set `{previous_tier}` to null (first run)
    63→
    64→**Read existing preferences.yaml** at `{project-root}/_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/preferences.yaml`:
    65→- If exists: check for `tier_override` value
    66→- If not found: set `{tier_override}` to null
    67→
    68→### 3. Verify Tool: ast-grep
    69→
    70→Run: `ast-grep --version`
    71→
    72→- If succeeds: record `{ast_grep: true}` and store version string
    73→- If fails (command not found or error): record `{ast_grep: false}`
    74→
    75→### 4. Verify Tool: gh
    76→
    77→Run: `gh --version`
    78→
    79→- If succeeds: record `{gh_cli: true}` and store version string
    80→- If fails: record `{gh_cli: false}`
    81→
    82→### 5. Verify Tool: qmd
    83→
    84→Run: `qmd status`
    85→
    86→- If succeeds and indicates operational: record `{qmd: true}`
    87→- If fails or indicates not initialized: record `{qmd: false}`
    88→
    89→### 6. Calculate Tier
    90→
    91→**If `{tier_override}` is set and valid (Quick, Forge, or Deep):**
    92→- Use `{tier_override}` as `{calculated_tier}`
    93→- Note that override is active for the report step
    94→
    95→**If no override, apply tier rules from {tierRulesData}:**
    96→- `{ast_grep}` AND `{gh_cli}` AND `{qmd}` all true → **Deep**
    97→- `{ast_grep}` true (regardless of gh/qmd) → **Forge**
    98→- Otherwise → **Quick**
    99→
   100→**If `{tier_override}` is set but invalid:** ignore it, use detected tier, flag for warning in report.
   101→
   102→### 7. Auto-Proceed
   103→
   104→"**Proceeding to write configuration...**"
   105→
   106→#### Menu Handling Logic:
   107→
   108→- After tier calculation is complete, immediately load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
   109→

   111→
   112→- This is an auto-proceed step with no user choices
   113→- Proceed directly to next step after detection and calculation
   114→
   115→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   116→
   117→ONLY WHEN all 3 tools have been verified and the tier has been calculated will you load and read fully `{nextStepFile}` to execute the configuration write step.
   118→
   119→---
   120→
   121→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   122→
   123→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   124→
   125→- All 3 tools checked via verification commands (not existence checks)
   126→- Existing forge-tier.yaml read for re-run comparison (if present)
   127→- Existing preferences.yaml read for tier_override (if present)
   128→- Tier correctly calculated from tool results or override applied
   129→- All results stored in context for step-02
   130→- Auto-proceeded to step-02
   131→
   132→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   133→
   134→- Skipping any tool verification
   135→- Using `which` or `command -v` instead of verification commands
   136→- Assuming tool availability without running the command
   137→- Writing files in this step (that is step-02)
   138→- Not checking for existing configuration on re-runs
   139→
   140→**Master Rule:** Every tool MUST be verified empirically. No assumptions, no shortcuts.

---

**Reviewing all changes across 10 commits on the dev branch**

This review covers cross-commit conflicts, missing propagation, validation gaps, documentation inconsistencies, and behavioral regressions. All findings have been confirmed by reading the actual current state of the files.

---

## Critical Issues

### Issue 1: quick-skill step-05-validate.md still checks old ADR-L v2 format and ~50-80 token target after the Vercel-aligned format redesign (#38)

**Confidence: 100**

The snippet format was redesigned in #38 to the Vercel-aligned indexed format. `quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md` was updated (line 102-110) to generate the new 6-line pipe-delimited format targeting `~80-120 tokens`. However, `quick-skill/steps-c/step-05-validate.md` was not updated and still validates against the old format:

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-05-validate.md`
Lines 107-110:
```
- [ ] **ADR-L v2 format** — name+path, description, exports, optional refs
- [ ] **First line** matches pattern: `{name}: {exports list}`
- [ ] **Second line** matches pattern: `  → {path}/SKILL.md`
- [ ] **Approximate token count** is ~50-80 tokens
```

Every quick-skill snippet will now fail step-05 validation because:
1. The first-line pattern (`{name}: {exports list}`) no longer matches the new format (`[{skill_name} v{version}]|root: skills/{skill_name}/`)
2. The second-line pattern (`  → {path}/SKILL.md`) no longer matches (`|IMPORTANT: ...`)
3. The token target (~50-80) is wrong (new target is ~80-120)

The step-05-validate.md must be updated to check for the new Vercel-aligned format structure.

---

### Issue 2: quick-skill step-04-compile.md goal and success metrics still reference "ADR-L v2 format" after #38

**Confidence: 95**

Although step-04-compile.md's Section 3 was updated with the new snippet template, the step description field, the step goal text, the "Load skill template" sub-section, and the success metrics all still say "ADR-L v2 format":

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md`
- Line 13: "context-snippet.md in ADR-L v2 format"
- Line 61: "context-snippet.md ADR-L v2 format"
- Line 195 (SUCCESS): "context-snippet.md generated in correct ADR-L v2 format"

An LLM reading this step will get contradictory instructions: the goal says "ADR-L v2 format" but Section 3 shows the new Vercel-aligned template. The success metric further reinforces the old format label.

---

### Issue 3: create-stack-skill step-07-generate-output.md still generates the old ADR-L v2 format for context-snippet.md after #38

**Confidence: 100**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md`

Lines 102-106 still instruct generation in the old format:
```
Use ADR-L v2 format targeting ~50-80 tokens per library entry:
**{library}** ({import_count} imports): {top_3_exports}
  → integrates with: {comma-separated integration partners}
```

This is a write step — it produces the actual output file. The create-stack-skill workflow was not listed in #38's "7 files" but it generates context-snippet.md and is consumed by export-skill step-04-update-context.md, which rebuilds the complete CLAUDE.md managed section from all `context-snippet.md` files. Stack skills will produce snippets in the old format while single skills produce the new format, causing a mixed managed section that export-skill's rebuild assembles together.

---

### Issue 4: create-stack-skill step-08-validate.md validates against "ADR-L v2 format" after #38

**Confidence: 100**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-08-validate.md`

Line 142:
```
### 8. Validate context-snippet.md

Verify context-snippet.md follows ADR-L v2 format:
```

This validation step would wrongly flag the new Vercel-aligned format as invalid if the step-07 were also updated. And if step-07 is NOT updated (Issue 3), the validation would pass — but the generated file would be wrong format for the managed section rebuild in export-skill.

---

### Issue 5: setup-forge step-01-detect-and-tier.md references a non-existent step-06

**Confidence: 100**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/setup-forge/steps-c/step-01-detect-and-tier.md`

Line 96:
```
This is informational only — security scan availability does NOT affect the tier level. It is recorded in forge-tier.yaml so that step-06 (validate) can report actionable guidance when security scanning is unavailable.
```

The setup-forge workflow only has 4 steps (step-01 through step-04). There is no step-06 in setup-forge. The `security_scan` field is recorded in forge-tier.yaml, but the documentation is wrong about who reads it. The actual consumer is create-skill's `step-06-validate.md` (a different workflow), not a setup-forge step-06. This was apparently introduced as part of #39 (Snyk Agent Scan setup).

## Important Issues

### Issue 6: export-skill step-05-token-report.md still reports "~50-80 tokens per skill" benchmark after #38

The actual token target changed to ~80-120 tokens per `snippet-format.md` (line 6). This benchmark will consistently label valid snippets as exceeding the target, creating confusion for users who see "Current: ~100 tokens" against "Target: ~50-80 tokens".

### Issue 8: create-skill workflow.md description of step-06 is stale after tessl gate was added (#37)

**Confidence: 90**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/workflow.md`

This workflow is mostly autonomous with two strategic confirmation gates — one after ecosystem check (if a match is found) and one after source extraction (to confirm findings before compilation). All other steps auto-proceed.

After #37, step-06-validate is no longer always auto-proceed. It now conditionally halts for user input at section 6b when tessl returns suggestions. The workflow.md description still says "All other steps auto-proceed," which is wrong.

An LLM reading `workflow.md` first would have a wrong mental model of where user interaction can occur, potentially causing confusion if step-06 unexpectedly halts.

### Issue 12: export-skill step-03-generate-snippet.md has a self-contradictory format label (#38)

**Confidence: 90**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md`

The step generates the new Vercel-aligned format (lines 80-86 show the new template). However:

158:When you run `@Ferris EX` (export), SKF writes a managed section between `<!-- SKF:BEGIN -->` and `<!-- SKF:END -->` markers in your CLAUDE.md. Each entry includes a compressed description (the WHEN-TO-USE signal), top exports, and a reference file index (the WHERE-TO-LOOK signal) — ~50-80 tokens per skill. Your agent always knows what skills exist, what they do, and where to find detailed references — without loading the full skill until it's needed.

   200→## Dual-Output Strategy
   201→
   202→Based on [Vercel research](https://vercel.com/blog/agents-md-outperforms-skills-in-our-agent-evals): passive context (AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md) achieves 100% pass rate vs 53% for active skills alone.
   203→
   204→Every skill generates both:
   205→
   206→1. **SKILL.md** — Active skill loaded on trigger with full instructions
   207→2. **context-snippet.md** — Passive context, compressed index injected into CLAUDE.md
   208→
   209→### Managed CLAUDE.md Section
   210→
   211→Export injects a managed section between markers:
   212→
   213→```markdown
   214→<!-- SKF:BEGIN updated:2026-02-25 -->
   215→[SKF Skills]|3 skills|1 stack
   216→|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.
   217→|When using a listed library, read its SKILL.md before writing code.
   218→|
   219→|payment-service → skills/payment-service/
   220→|  Payment processing API — token management, session handling, webhooks
   221→|  exports: getToken, refreshToken, revokeSession, createSession
   222→|  refs: api-reference, type-definitions, webhook-patterns
   223→|
   224→|auth-service → skills/auth-service/
   225→|  Authentication service — session validation, user management, RBAC
   226→|  exports: getSession, validateToken, revokeSession, createUser
   227→|
   228→|my-project-stack → skills/my-project-stack/
   229→|  Full-stack integration patterns for Next.js + auth + DB + PWA
   230→|  stack: next@15, better-auth@3, spacetimedb@1, serwist@9
   231→|  integrations: auth↔db, pwa↔auth, gateway↔auth
   232→<!-- SKF:END -->
   233→```
   234→

Each entry includes a compressed description (the WHEN-TO-USE signal), top exports, and a reference file index (the WHERE-TO-LOOK signal) — ~50-80 tokens per skill.   "new_string": "When you run `@Ferris EX` (export), SKF writes a managed section between `<!-- SKF:BEGIN -->` and `<!-- SKF:END -->` markers in your CLAUDE.md. Each entry uses a Vercel-aligned indexed format: version pinning (so the agent knows training data may be stale), an explicit retrieval instruction (\"read SKILL.md before writing code\"), section anchors (`#quick-start`, `#key-types`) for direct navigation, and inline gotchas that prevent common mistakes without requiring a file read — ~80-120 tokens per skill. Aligned with [Vercel's research](https://vercel.com/blog/agents-md-outperforms-skills-in-our-agent-evals) finding that passive context with retrieval instructions outperforms active skills alone."
}

Each issue was found during execution, documented with workarounds, and fixed with full cross-workflow propagation.\n\n- **#30** Sparse checkout fails with glob patterns — convert to directory roots + `--skip-checks`\n- **#31** `gh release list --json body` unsupported — two-step fetch (list tags, then view each)\n- **#32** Brief version vs source version mismatch — auto-detection from manifests + reconciliation at extraction time (10 files)\n- **#33** `qmd embed` missing after `qmd collection add` — vector search was non-functional (5 files)\n- **#34** Doc fetcher returns minimal content from root URLs — subpage discovery via sitemap/map\n- **#35** Two-tier content penalized by tessl conciseness scorer — differentiation rules + expected score documentation\n- **#36** \"Files in scope\" ambiguous (total repo vs filtered) — clarified as filtered count\n- **#37** tessl suggestions silently skipped — conditional user decision gate with zero-hallucination safeguards\n- **#38** Context-snippet passive format redesigned to Vercel-aligned indexed structure (7 files, ~80-120 tokens)\n- **#39** Snyk Agent Scan setup undocumented — detection in setup-forge, guidance in step-06, README section\n\nPlus 2 follow-up commits from cross-commit review catching 12 propagation issues (stale format references, wrong token budgets, missing format updates in quick-skill/stack-skill validation).\n\n**Stats:** 35 files changed, +458 / -147 lines across 13 commits.
